<summary>Agent "Build calc.py pricing rules" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I built `calc.py` and its tests. All 34 tests pass (0.001 s) and ruff is clean at line length 120. I only created those two files: nothing else was touched, nothing was committed, and no browser or agent was run.

**Input models**
- `BidInput` holds the whole bid: `tender_reference`, `scenario_date`, the positions, an optional `approval`, `fx_rates` (FX key to EUR per unit), `metal_indices` (code to dated rates), an optional `setup_pool` and an optional `project_bonus`.
- `PositionInput` carries one position:
  - its id and final quantity in the stated unit, with `unit` and `pieces_per_set` for Satz;
  - `material`, where `None` means no matching material and leads to `no_bid`;
  - the candidate price `conditions`, the Konditionsgruppe rule (`discount_scope`: base only, base plus surcharge, or none), the ordinary `setup_fee`, the `packaging` tariff and the delivery `commitment`.
- `Material` has stock, both lead times, carton size, allocation mass, surcharge mass and the metal index code.
- The smaller records are `PriceCondition` / `PriceScale`, `MetalIndexRate`, `PackagingTariff`, `DeliveryCommitment`, `SetupPool`, `BonusBand` / `ProjectBonus` and `Approval` (percent plus optional threshold).

**Public functions**
- `calculate(bid) -&gt; BidResult` does the whole calculation. `BidResult.answer(notes=None)` turns it into the answer-schema dict. The result also keeps `order_value`, `bonus_total`, `missing_materials` and a per-position `debug` list of unrounded intermediates.
- Selection helpers for the rules the spec defines exactly:
  - `resolve_quantity` and `to_pieces` / `parse_unit` (rules 1, 13, 17);
  - `apply_amendments`, `nominal_stroke` and `matching_materials` (rules 14, 20, 3);
  - `latest_authoritative` (rule 21);
  - `valid_condition` and `reached_scale` (rule 4), `metal_rate` (rule 28).
- Calculation helpers: `effective_discount`, `bonus_credit`, `allocate_cents`, `lead_time`, `money`, `position_key`.

**Assumptions to check**
- **Setup pool cent rule (25):** the mail states the rule, so it is a parameter. The default is largest remainder. The other options are rounding each share, or giving the leftover cent to the largest, first or last member. The base can be half-up or floor, and ties go by ascending or descending position.
- **Position order** for ties is "natural", so "2" comes before "10".
- **Project bonus (26):** a position's goods value is final pieces × the EUR base price, undiscounted. A flag `include_surcharge` adds the mass surcharge if the mail says so. The bonus is split by largest remainder with ties by ascending position.
- **Base price:** it is rounded to cents even for EUR prices. This matters when the price unit is 100.
- **Discount threshold (16):** the order value is final pieces × rounded EUR base, summed over all positions. On `no_bid`, a conditional approval gives 0 % but an unconditional one keeps its percent.
- **Metal index:** rates are taken as EUR per kg. If a material has no index code and exactly one index exists, that one is used. A single rate without a window applies as is.
- **Packaging:** a tariff is a flat EUR price per carton. A position with no tariff reports packaging as 0.00.
- **Other matching details:**
  - Q&amp;A changes and amendments dated after the scenario date are ignored.
  - Numeric attributes match as numbers ("50.0" equals 50); text matches ignore case and extra whitespace.
  - If several price conditions are valid on the date, the one with the latest start wins.
- **Missing facts:** a missing FX rate, metal rate, scale, valid condition or carton size raises `CalcError` rather than guessing. The orchestrator needs to catch it, for example to leave that field blank or skip the position.

The tests use only invented values (T-0001, M-100, example.com) with the hand calculations in comments. They cover rules 1, 3–8, 12–17, 20–28 and the answer format, including the case where rounding each component first would give a different price.

Files are in /Users/jonasstenglein/OtherProjects/EHL_Finale/error404ai_ehl_finals/agent:
- calc.py
- tests/test_calc.py</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: keep it, with changes.** Labelling each test run and submission with a version, an experiment name and a note is useful, and the platform doesn't do it. Three parts cost more than they give for a one-day, five-person repo: the committed generated table, committed local runs, and the "always" rule.

What I checked: `bash -n` passes on both wrappers. `python3 dev/track.py report` reproduces `docs/experiments.md` byte for byte. `atira test-run ./agent --dry-run` showed that `.gitignore` keeps `traces/` out of the uploaded package. I edited nothing.

**Top changes, ranked**

1. **Stop committing `docs/experiments.md`'s table.** Every regeneration rewrites the whole table, so two people who both record will conflict on every merge. Gitignore the generated table, or print it to stdout, and move "What we tried and learned" into its own hand-written file such as `docs/learned.md`. That file is the most valuable piece of the setup.
2. **Add `experiments/runs.jsonl merge=union` to `.gitattributes`.** Two teammates appending lines always conflict at end of file. With union, git keeps both sides' lines.
3. **Commit only test runs and submissions; keep local runs local.** Local runs are frequent and unscored, and they score nothing that would show what works. Record them to a gitignored file, or only when they taught something.
4. **Weaken "Tracking runs (always)" in CLAUDE.md.** Make it: record every test run and submission, and add a bullet to the learned file when a run taught something. As written, another person's Claude session will churn the log on every debug run.
5. **Don't rely on the git commit to pin the code.** `atira test-run` packs untracked files too: right now `agent/evidence.py`, `research*.py` and others are uploaded but in no commit. Both the commit and the `dirty` flag are taken when you record, not when the run started; for `test-run.sh` that is after `--follow` ends, so a pull in between records the wrong commit. `atira package &lt;id&gt;` already returns the exact uploaded package, so record that (the sha256) instead.

**Redundancy with the platform:** `atira status`, `atira package` and the run pages already hold the score, breakdown, cost, error and code. What the setup adds is labels and notes. That supports the 90% version: a roughly 30-line `track.py platform`, the union-merged jsonl, and the hand-written learnings file.

**Bugs and fragile spots**

- `run-local.sh` uses `uv run --no-sync`, so it won't install dependencies on a fresh clone. It also brings back the stale-venv problem already in the log (the httpx crash). Drop `--no-sync` or run `uv sync` first.
- `local` takes the newest trace by modification time. If the agent dies before creating a trace, the duplicate-id check aborts the recording instead of saving the wrong run. If two runs overlap, the wrong trace is recorded. Pass `--trace` explicitly.
- A relative `TRACE_DIR` resolves against `agent/` in the agent but against the repo root in `track.py`, so they look in different places.
- In `test-run.sh`, Ctrl-C during `--follow` loses the record with no hint, while the run keeps going on the platform.
- Windows: these are POSIX `sh` scripts calling `python3`, so they need WSL or Git Bash. They work on macOS and Linux, and `track.py` works on the system Python 3.9.
- `atira models proxy` is only mentioned in a comment. If it isn't running, the run fails late instead of the wrapper checking first.

**Privacy and fair play**

- `overrides()` records every variable starting with `AGENT_`, `LLM_`, `THINKING` or `MAX_STEPS` into a committed file. Today's `QWEN_API_KEY` and `CLEF_API_KEY` don't match, but a future `LLM_API_KEY` would be committed. Use an explicit allowlist or redact anything matching `KEY|TOKEN|SECRET`.
- Local `outcome.confirmation` is model text read from the site and could carry site values.
- Fair play looks fine: `dev/`, `experiments/` and `docs/` are outside `agent/`, the packer honours `.gitignore`, and the current notes contain no credentials or site values.

The files involved are `dev/track.py`, `dev/run-local.sh`, `dev/test-run.sh`, `experiments/runs.jsonl`, `docs/experiments.md` and the "Tracking runs" section of `CLAUDE.md`, all under `/Users/jonasstenglein/OtherProjects/EHL_Finale/error404ai_ehl_finals/`.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: likely pass for the package committed at HEAD.** I found no scenario values, no fixed click path, no crawler and no secrets. It only talks to the platform, the run's sites and the model proxy, and in the sandbox it attaches to the provided browsers rather than starting its own. The real risks are in the untracked work-in-progress files.

**Committed package at HEAD, ranked by severity**

1. **Low to medium flag risk: the answer strategy is fixed in code** (`submit.py:63`, `81-91`). The bid goal hardcodes `decision: bid`, every position left out (Position auslassen), discount 0 and total 0,00. None of these are copied scenario values, but rule 11 says a model must "make the important decisions", and here the decision and the money fields are fixed. A reviewer may read this as a stub or a pre-decided answer. Fix: once v2 lands, let a model choose bid or no_bid from the evidence. Until then the docstring's frank "v1" wording is fine; don't add notes arguing it is compliant.
2. **Low: the local browser launch has a weak guard** (`browsers.py:28`, `45-46`). `chromium.launch` is skipped only when `CDP_URLS`/`CDP_URL` are set, so if they were ever missing in the sandbox it would start an own browser. Fix: also refuse to launch when `CHALLENGE_RUN_TOKEN` is set.
3. **Low: the generic helpers in `browser_tools.py` all look fine.**
   - Auto login (`:173`) fills the run's own credentials.
   - Cookie handling (`:196`) prefers the necessary-only answer.
   - The popup closer (`:25-57`) only presses close buttons and never follows links.
   - The error-page reload (`:229`) is a retry.
   - These all fit "helpers may retry and fill forms". The human-check helper (`:61-83`, `:215`) sets sliders and ticks robot checkboxes by script. That is the one thing a strict reviewer could call circumventing a site control. It is narrowly scoped (other checkboxes are left to the model) and reported to the model. Optionally drop it and let the model use `fill` on the slider.
   - `_on_dialog` (`:137`) accepts every browser confirm. Acceptable, because the model triggered the action.
4. **Cosmetic:**
   - `tests/test_kit.py:24` uses the public-1 siteId `tender-portal` in a fixture. Rename it to something like `site-a`.
   - `tests/` launches a local Chromium, but only as tests, never in the run.
   - `README.md` is still the starter-kit text and steers nothing.
   - `calc.py` holds only rule mechanics and placeholder data.

**Untracked files (risky once committed)**

1. **Medium (likely flagged): text copied from site pages into prompts.** These phrases are not in the brief, so they most likely come from public-1 pages:
   - `research_tender.py:270-271`: "Kein Rüstpool", "kein Projektbonus".
   - `research_mail.py:208`: decoy labels "Entwurf", "nicht versandt", "Keine Freigabe".
   - Fix: describe these generically ("statements of non-membership", "draft or unsent previews").
2. **Medium (flag risk under rule 11): code decides what to investigate and what to choose.**
   - `research_mail.py:173-189` plus `research_kind` send nine search tasks from a fixed list. Models still drive each search, but the plan itself is code.
   - `research.py` `select_material` picks the material in code.
   - Fix: let an orchestrating model decide which kinds of record to pursue from the brief and Q&amp;A, and let the model choose the material while code only validates the exact match.
3. **Low to medium: sender names and subjects hardcoded from the public brief** (`research_mail.py:174-188`). Strings like `leitung`, `kalkulation` and the subject words do appear in the brief, so they are allowed. But they are copied from public-1's brief, and the private run may phrase them differently. Fix: have `briefing.py` pull the sender-to-subject mapping out of the run's brief.
4. **Low: off-site navigation without a host check** (`research.py:667`, `718`). `_goto(urljoin(site url, link))` follows links reported by the model and does not check the host, unlike `goto` and `open_pages`. Fix: reuse the same-host check.

German words from the brief (Hauptangebot, Position auslassen, Kataloggruppe, Werkpost) are used as the brief intends, so they are fine.</result>

<summary>Agent "Investigate isolated site state options" finished</summary>

<result>## Isolated state on the challenge sites: findings

The platform gives teams no isolated scouting state, but most of it isn't needed: the bid wizard is already private to each browser session. The only shared state is the webmail, which holds the TAN mails.

**1. What the docs and API offer: nothing per team.**
- `api.md`: scouting returns "the logins the organizers publish… These are demo logins without a run token… nothing done with them counts." The response has `until_revoked: true` and never expires.
- The OpenAPI spec has no reset endpoint, no extra accounts and no per-team scouting. The only related endpoint is `POST /v1/challenges/{id}/practice-runs`, marked "Deprecated and internal: organizers' demo access… participants never need it" (llms-full: "Organizers only"). I did not call it.
- `faq.md`: "Can I run my agent outside the sandbox? While you develop, yes, anywhere, with the scouting logins."
- The two announcements say nothing about this. One raised concurrent test runs to 4, but our learned.md says 5; worth checking.

**2. Experiment (two separate headless browser contexts, no other agent running):**
- **Bid wizard: tied to the session cookie, not the account.** Fresh contexts A and B both opened an empty step 1 (Bieterdaten). A filled in the bidder data once (the brief's company name and VAT id, plus the mailbox address read from the webmail) and pressed Weiter. After a reload, A showed step 2 (Angebotsart) and B still showed an empty step 1. The leftover state we saw earlier came from reusing our own session.
- **Webmail: one mailbox shared by everyone.** Both contexts saw exactly the same list: 66 messages, the newest being TAN mails numbered "Anforderung 66, 65, 64…". So the TAN mails and their numbering are shared across all teams.
- **Untested:** whether one TAN request cancels TANs requested from other sessions, because I was not allowed to request one. The shared numbering and the jumps we saw make account-wide cancellation likely.

**3. Using a test run's logins from my laptop: technically possible, not advisable. I did not try it.**
- A past test run's log shows the same public hostnames as scouting (`atira-rfq-*.kyora.run`). Runs get separate logins on the same public sites, so they would probably be reachable from outside.
- The rules work against it:
  - run-contract: "Site logins are issued per run and only work while that run is live."
  - fair-play forbids "trying to get data out of the sandbox" and says "Nobody helps it during a run."
- Our own agent would also share those accounts while the run is live, and code that prints `CHALLENGE_SITE_ACCESS` is exactly what the package reviewer flags.
- Recommendation: don't do it without written permission from the organizers.

## Options, best first

1. **Use the session isolation we already have (no risk).** Run the bid form locally up to step 6, in separate browser contexts per developer. Add a stop before the TAN request, and test TAN signing only in test runs (up to 4–5 at once).
2. **Ask the organizers (one message, low risk).** Ask for per-team scouting logins or a mailbox reset, and whether using a live test run's logins from outside is allowed. Mention that TAN requests from different teams collide in the shared mailbox.
3. **Read the TAN by request number right after our own request.** This keeps local signing mostly working, but we still cancel other teams' TANs and they cancel ours. Use it sparingly.
4. **Leak a test run's logins to a laptop (not recommended).** It risks a fair-play violation and collides with our own running agent.

I appended one bullet with these results to `docs/learned.md` and committed nothing. The probe script and its logs are in `/Users/jonasstenglein/.claude/jobs/064ce75e/tmp/iso/`: `observe.py`, `obs1.log` and `obs2.log`.</result>

<summary>Agent "v2 spec and ERP research workers" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I built the spec and ERP research workers. On public-1 the last three runs gave correct records for all five requirements, but end-to-end time is 188 to 318 s against your ~3 min (180 s) target. Nothing is committed; I only created new files.

**Files**
- `agent/research.py`: both workers, the two read-only tools, and the `Erp` class (runs requirements and materials in parallel, one tab each).
- `agent/evidence.py`: the record models, deterministic parsers, the exact-match validator `check_choice`, and the mapping to calc (`to_position_input` builds `calc.PositionInput` with `calc.Material` and `calc.PriceCondition` inside).
- `dev/research_check.py`: the local runner.

**How to run:** `eval "$(atira scout --env)"; HEADLESS=1 uv run --project agent python dev/research_check.py [--reserve KEY=MM ...] [--only A-3]`. Without `--reserve`, the two "nutzbarer Weg" requirements stay pending (their research is still collected).

**How it works**
- **Which site is which:** a model reads the brief (`research_sites`); nothing branches on siteId or URL.
- **Spec:**
  - With thinking off, a worker finds the document, passes the access gate and lists the revisions with their statuses.
  - A thinking-on call picks the current revision; a validator checks it is one of the listed revisions.
  - The worker then opens that revision and calls `read_document` with the "next page" control it chose. That tool pages through and stops when a page repeats.
  - Each page is extracted in parallel and checked against the page text. Requirements are handed to the ERP stage as soon as they are parsed.
- **ERP:**
  - A warm-up worker signs in during the spec stage and runs again if the session sat idle too long.
  - Per requirement, a worker searches the Kataloggruppe and lists every hit with its attributes. Every hit is then inspected.
  - Per material, a worker opens the views it chooses (`open_pages` only opens links it has already seen). A reader turns the kept views into one record, checked against the view text. If facts are missing, it does a second pass that keeps what was already read.
  - A thinking-on model chooses the material. `check_choice` (active status plus `calc.matching_materials`) sends a wrong choice back with the reason.

**Coordinator's fair-play points, all addressed:** the model now chooses the material and code only validates it; links followed from model reports go through the same host check as `goto`; site wording is out of the prompts, and docstring examples use invented values.

**Records produced on public-1 (match what I checked by hand):**
- Current revision R-6156 ("gültig" in a tooltip), not R-4034, which opens first. All five requirements read correctly; A-2 and A-4 are "nutzbarer Weg" with keys ER-4001 and ER-9526; A-5 is 1 Satz = 5 Stück.
- A-1 → HZ-1609 (HZ-8405 is blocked): free 31 (blocked 68 not counted), lead time 2 weeks from stock / 7 production, 11 per carton, allocation 3.1 kg, surcharge 2.125 kg, MET-4607, EUR per 1: 25+ 2743.58, 10+ 2930.65, 1+ 3117.71.
- A-3 → HZ-9275 (HZ-9704 is 220 bar). A-5 → HZ-6932 (HZ-3567 has stroke 325), price unit 100 with en-US numbers parsed correctly.
- A-2 → HZ-2170 holds two condition records (2026 and 2027), USD with FX-6282, price unit 100 vs 1. A-4 → HZ-3916, price unit 100, de-DE numbers.
- The A-2/A-4 choices only came out because I passed reserves 5.5 and 12 on the command line. Those are invented values that fit the ERP strokes, used only to exercise the choice step; the real values must come from the Technikfreigabe mail.

**Timings (end to end, with brief reading):**

| Run | Total | Spec | ERP stage | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | 188 s | ~73 s | 96 s | no session drops |
| 6 | 318 s | ~93 s | 216 s | ERP session dropped mid-run |
| 7 (final code) | 252 s | ~70 s | 169 s | one search retry |

Model steps take about 2 s at the median, but hosted calls sometimes hang.

**Accuracy and speed problems seen**
- **ERP session drops:** the ERP ended the whole session mid-run twice, after about 85 s and about 130 s, and every tab then signed in again at once and failed ("Formular abgelaufen"). The scouting account is shared, so a sign-in from another process probably causes this; a real run has its own logins. The retry pass recovered every fact.
- **Wasted steps:** without thinking, navigation workers sometimes keep refining a search that already has hits, or guess record pages that don't exist. Step limits (14 for search, 12 for detail) and one retry contain this, but it costs 30 to 60 s.
- **Gap filling:** without data, the reader once filled in 0 or "unknown"; the prompt now forbids that and the completeness check retries.
- **Spec dates:** the revision dates flip between day-first and month-first on every load, so I don't report them and the decision goes by the status the site marks.
- **Earlier hang:** one early run had a 97 s stall, which looked like proxy hangs plus the 30 s client timeout.

**Harness changes I recommend (not made)**
1. **llm.py:** a per-call timeout. Thinking calls run 16 to 35 s and get killed at 30 s and retried. I work around it in `_slow_model()` by raising `LLM_TIMEOUT` (default 90 s) while the model is built.
2. **browser_tools.py:** serialise sign-in per site, one lock per site shared by all tabs of a context, so a dropped session doesn't trigger N parallel logins.
3. **browser_tools.py:** a public `SiteSession.goto(url)` with the host check, and a hook that records each view, so I can stop wrapping `session.look` and calling `_goto`.
4. **worker.py:** a `validators=` parameter on `run_worker`, and recording the grounding refusals (`output_refused`) the workers hit.

**Hooks for the other workers**
- `effective_requirement(req, amendments, reserves, scenario_date)` applies scoped amendments and the nominal-stroke rule.
- `await reselect(effective_reqs, erp_result)` re-runs the model's choice without browsing, so the material can be decided once reserves arrive.

I stopped my scouting daemon by PID. On the processes the coordinator raised: my only pattern kill was `pkill -f rscout/daemon.py`, which only matches that daemon's path.</result>

<summary>Agent "Evaluate and integrate Clef decisions" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>I ran an offline comparison of Clef and Qwen on real page views, and Clef only wins at one decision: spotting an error or dead-end page after an action. I added that one check behind a switch that is off by default. It hasn't been tested inside a full agent run: our only end-to-end flow asks for a TAN, which I was told not to do.

## How I measured
I hand-labelled 68 decisions on 55 distinct page views from the trace files, ran each model on them 3 times, and scored accuracy, latency and simulated cost. The labelled data contains site text, so it stays local and gitignored in `dev/clef_eval/data/`. Only the eval script and a results summary are committed.

| Decision type | Model | Accuracy | Median / p90 s | $ per decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Which link or control to use (14 items) | Clef | 79 % | 1.4 / 2.0 | 0.00028 |
| | Qwen, thinking off | **93 %** | 1.3 / 2.0 | 0.00013 |
| | Qwen, thinking on | 86 % | 6.4 / 106 (one call hung) | 0.00044 |
| Right document / revision / record (16 items) | Clef | 88 % | 1.1 / 1.6 | 0.00028 |
| | Qwen, thinking off | 92 % | 1.1 / 2.0 | 0.00013 |
| | Qwen, thinking on | **98 %** | 5.2 / 10.5 | 0.00044 |
| Did the action work / error or decoy page (20 items) | Clef | **100 %** | 1.1 / 1.6 | 0.00015 |
| | Qwen, thinking off | 93 % | 1.4 / 2.4 | 0.00008 |
| | Qwen, thinking on (first 14 items only) | 100 % | 4.3 / 10.3 | 0.00044 |
| Pre-filtering list rows (5 lists, 18 rows) | All three | 100 % | Clef 1.1, Qwen off 1.2, Qwen on 5.7 | per list |

All the costs are tiny next to a worker step, so accuracy and speed are what matter.

## Where Clef helps and where it doesn't
- **Error or dead-end pages: Clef wins.** It got all 60 right, and its yes/no probabilities were well apart (at least 0.68 for dead ends, at most 0.09 for real pages), at the same ~1 s as Qwen without thinking. Qwen without thinking missed an empty reading pane every time and once misjudged a "TAN requested" page. Qwen with thinking is also exact but about 4x slower.
- **Choosing a link: Clef loses.** It fails when the choice depends on order or a row's tooltip, such as the highest request number, the newest mail being on the previous page, or the revision marked valid only in a tooltip.
- **Right document or record: Clef loses.** It took a `&lt;ref&gt;-A` look-alike for the exact tender reference and misread a stated quantity. Qwen without thinking also fell for the look-alike.
- **Pre-filtering lists: no gain.** All three were perfect. Clef can answer many yes/no questions in one call, but Qwen already handles short lists in one step.
- Clef gives the same answers on every repeat; Qwen without thinking varies between repeats.

## What I integrated
- **Switch:** `AGENT_CLEF=1` turns it on (`USE_CLEF=1` also works). I used the `AGENT_` prefix because `dev/track.py` already records those variables, so `AGENT_CLEF=1 dev/run-local.sh v1 "&lt;note&gt;" clef-pagecheck` would log the A/B run.
- **New module `agent/clef.py`:** the Clef call with retries, a concurrency cap and token counting. It also has:
  - a `page_check(last_action)` tool that workers can call to ask whether the current page is a dead end;
  - an `outcome_hint(view, action)` helper for an automatic check after actions, not wired in yet;
  - the link-choice and yes/no helpers the eval uses.
- **Hook already in place:** `agent/worker.py` gets the import plus `tools=[*browser, *tools, *clef.tools()]` in `run_worker`. With the switch off this adds nothing.
- **Hooks I only describe:** those files belong to the other agents.
  - `browser_tools.py`: in `SiteSession.act` and `goto`, after `look()`, put `await clef.outcome_hint(self.view, f"{kind} {ref}")` above the view. This costs about 1 s per action, so it's better limited to searches, opening messages and submits.
  - `submit.py`: `await clef.dead_end(view, "entered the TAN and submitted")` would catch a rejected TAN quickly.
- **`agent.json`:** still lists only `"qwen"`. Add `"clef"` to `"models"` before shipping with the switch on. The sandbox can't take run variables, so a sandbox A/B also needs the default flipped in code.

**Branch and commit:** `worktree-agent-a0f71dbdbb8caf177`, one commit `c0b62bf` "Add Clef page check behind AGENT_CLEF" (author josteng, nothing under `.claude/`, not pushed). The repo's Entire hook added an `Entire-Checkpoint` line to the commit message; I left it. The 55 unit tests pass. I added one bullet to `docs/learned.md`.

## Expected answer
VG-2027-7093, bid, EUR, discount 5 %, total_net **356949.62**.

| Pos | Material | Qty | Base | Surcharge | Packaging | Rebate | Unit price | Lead time (weeks) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01.01 | HZ-1609 | 25 | 2743.58 | 6.69 | 1.59 | 0.00 | 2614.68 | 2 |
| 01.02 | HZ-2170 | 29 | 705.15 | 5.91 | 4.57 | 0.00 | 680.37 | 3 |
| 01.03 | HZ-2170 | 16 | 753.23 | 12.57 | 4.97 | 580.41 | 734.49 | 11 |
| 01.04 | HZ-3916 | 21 | 2107.18 | 5.25 | 1.26 | 2131.11 | 1906.85 | 3 |
| 01.05 | HZ-6932 | 130 | 1866.15 | 8.06 | 1.94 | 11683.58 | 1692.74 | 3 |

The traps this answer depends on:
- **01.01 quantity:** Q&amp;A 3916 is dated after 8148, so 25 (not 26), even though its number is lower.
- **01.01 material:** HZ-8405 matches too but is blocked (status 03).
- **01.05 quantity:** Q&amp;A 8152 (32 Satz) is withdrawn by 8153, so 26 Satz × 5 = 130 pieces.
- **A-2 stroke:** the latest amendment gives 482 usable; adding 2 × 9 mm reserve gives 500 nominal.
- **A-4 stroke:** 576 usable plus 2 × 12 mm reserve gives 600 nominal.
- **Price units and locales:** the USD and EUR conditions are per 100 pieces, and "64,853 (en-US)" means 64853.
- **Valid records:** the FX rate is 1.0873 (the 1.25 mail is future-dated) and the metal index is 3.15, the window containing 25.01.
- **01.01 delivery:** pooled demand 25 exceeds the commitment ceiling of 24, so stock delivery applies (2 weeks).
- **01.03 delivery:** pooled HZ-2170 demand is 45 against a ceiling of 44, so production (11 weeks).
- **01.02 delivery:** the ceiling is 45, inclusive, so the 3-week commitment applies.

## Fields I'm unsure about
- **01.05 surcharge_per_unit:** rounding the whole per-piece sum once gives 8.06, which I used. Rounding each component separately gives 8.05. This is the only field where the two readings differ; `--alt` accepts 8.05.
- **How the two extra checks map to fields (guess):** I scored `withdrawn_answer` as the 01.05 quantity, and `index_window` as the surcharge_per_unit of all five positions.
- **Arithmetic consistency (guess):** I required every position to be present and total_net to equal the sum of quantity × unit price. v1 rules out a plain sum check: an empty answer with total 0 would pass it, yet the platform gave 0.
- **Extra or duplicate positions:** each costs one position share (13 points), with position points floored at 0.

## agent/calc.py
I fed it the same facts and its output matches my independent Decimal derivation on every field. I found no bug that changes public-1. Three risks for other scenarios:
- **Day-only date checks** (`calc.py:263`, `:294`, `:338`): records are compared by date only. A record dated the same day but later than the scenario time (for example 25.01 at 10:00 when the scenario is 09:00) would be accepted.
- **Undated withdrawals** (`calc.py:259-266`): `resolve_quantity` honours a withdrawal even if the withdrawal itself is future-dated.
- **Overlapping metal-index windows** (`calc.py:365`): if two windows contain the date, the last-listed rate wins.

## Using the scorer
```sh
uv run --project agent python checks/derive.py         # rebuilds expected_public1.json, cross-checks calc.py
python3 checks/score.py &lt;answer.json&gt; [--alt] [--json]  # per-field table and quality out of 100
```
It accepts the plain answer object or one wrapped as `{"answer": {...}}`, and money as numbers or German-format strings. Blank or missing fields score 0.

## Why most timing came from test runs, not local runs
After your update about shared scouting logins I moved the full sign-and-TAN measurements to test runs. Local runs had shown a correct TAN being rejected because another team's request invalidated it.
- Local runs now only cover the form phase. `AGENT_STOP_BEFORE_SIGN=1` stops before any TAN request, so we don't invalidate other teams' TANs.
- One of my earlier full local runs did request TANs on the shared webmail before your warning.

## Timings per variant (test runs)

| Variant | Duration | Quality | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| Baseline (all thinking on) | 201 s | 10 | $0.019 |
| A: form thinking off, review thinking on | 271 s (1 run) | 10 | $0.019 |
| B: thinking off in both phases | 171 s (1 run) | 10 | $0.012 |
| **Final variant** (4 runs) | **94, 96, 105, 87 s** | 10 each | $0.008 to $0.010 |

Run A was slow because of three problems, all fixed in the final variant:
- The model went off to read every tender document (35 s). The goal now says to tick the acknowledgement without opening them.
- The mail lookup hit its 8-step limit once. It now reloads the inbox first, gets a hint to open the message's own page, and has 10 steps.
- Some thinking-on steps took 20 to 34 s.

On the local scouting sites, the form phase alone (stopping before the TAN) took 41 to 52 s with the final code, against about 126 s before.

## What changed
- **`briefing.py` and `llm.py`:** the briefing runs with thinking off and is still validated against the brief: 6 to 10 s instead of 17 to 45 s. `llm.thinking(phase, default)` reads `THINKING_BRIEF`, `THINKING_FORM`, `THINKING_REVIEW` and `THINKING_SIGN`, so experiments are recorded.
- **`agent.py`:** all sites are signed in while the brief is being read.
- **Bid worker in `submit.py`**, which now runs in three steps:
  - **Form phase:** thinking off, up to the review/signing page. The contact email is looked up first and put straight into the goal.
  - **Summary check:** one model call compares the review page with the requirements.
  - **Signing phase:** thinking stays off when the check passes, and turns on if the check fails or finds problems.
- **TAN hand-off:** a new `request_signing_tan(ref)` tool clicks the button the model chose, then fetches the TAN. It also passes the portal's reply (e.g. "Anforderung N") to the mail worker so it picks that exact message. The bid worker can't request another TAN while a lookup runs.
- **Fewer steps:** a new `fill_in(actions)` tool lets the model do several fills, selects, ticks or clicks on one page in one step; switch it off with `AGENT_FILL_IN=0`. I did not turn on parallel tool calls: element refs are renumbered after every view, so batched clicks could hit the wrong button.
- **Clean exit:** once the bid is in, the platform closes the run's model token and the last model call gets a 401. That error is now caught; the score was never affected.

## Correctness checks
- **Summary check accuracy:** I tested it offline on 6 edited copies of a real review page: one correct, plus an alternative offer, a 5 % discount, a position not omitted, an extra position and a transposed VAT id. With thinking off it judged all 6 correctly in 2 to 5 s; with thinking on, also all 6, but in 4 to 27 s. So it defaults to off.
- **Wrong clicks:** with thinking off, the form phase sometimes clicks "Position hinzufügen" (add position) instead of "Weiter". It recovered in every run and the scores show no extra positions, but it costs steps.

## Recommended defaults (already in the code)
- `THINKING_BRIEF=0`, `THINKING_FORM=0`, `THINKING_REVIEW=0`, `THINKING_SIGN` on only when the check doesn't pass.
- `AGENT_FILL_IN=1`.

No changes to `worker.py`, `browser_tools.py` or `extract.py` were needed; the new tools live in `submit.py`. Lessons are added as bullets in `docs/learned.md`, and all test runs are recorded in `experiments/runs.jsonl`.</result>

## Result

Branch `worktree-agent-a1517d68c93e50e27`, commit `2a5267d` ("Compare record times, not days, in calc rules"), author `josteng &lt;jonas@stenglein.dev&gt;`, `Entire-Checkpoint:` trailer kept by the hook, nothing pushed, nothing under `.claude/` staged. All paths below are under `/Users/jonasstenglein/OtherProjects/EHL_Finale/error404ai_ehl_finals/.claude/worktrees/agent-a1517d68c93e50e27/`.

### Fixes in `agent/calc.py`

**Shared timestamp semantics (lines 31-72).** New helpers `_instant` (naive stamps count as UTC), `_order` (total order over dates and timestamps, a date sorts as its UTC midnight), `_not_later(value, cutoff)` (two timestamps compare as instants; anything involving a date-only value compares by calendar day), and a `Stamp = Annotated[date | datetime, BeforeValidator(_parse_stamp)]` field type. The validator keeps `"2026-05-15"` a `date` and `"2026-05-15T09:00:00Z"` an aware `datetime` (plain pydantic would turn a midnight `T00:00:00Z` string into a date). `BidInput.scenario_date`, `QuantityChange.date`, `Amendment.date`, `DatedRecord.date`, `PriceCondition.valid_from/valid_to` and `MetalIndexRate.valid_from/valid_to` are now `Stamp`; existing `date`/`datetime` callers work unchanged.

1. **Same-day-but-later records (risk 1)**: `resolve_quantity` (line 129-144), `apply_amendments` (line 160-178) and `latest_authoritative` (line 196-208) now filter with `_not_later(record.date, scenario_date)` and pick the latest with `_order(...)`, so a 10:00Z record does not apply to a 09:00Z scenario, a `+02:00` offset is honoured, and a date-only record on the scenario day still applies. `latest_authoritative` covers approvals (16/21), FX fixing, packaging, commitments, setup pool, bonus and metal-index mails; `Approval`/`fx_rates` carry no dates themselves, so no further change was needed there.
2. **Future-dated withdrawals (risk 2)**: new `Withdrawal(number, date, withdraws)` model (line 122). `resolve_quantity` accepts `Iterable[str | Withdrawal]` for `withdrawn`; a `Withdrawal` only removes its target when `_not_later(w.date, scenario_date)`; bare strings keep the old meaning (already dated by the caller), so the `set[str]` API is backward compatible.
3. **Overlapping metal windows (risk 3)**: `metal_rate` (line 233-256) no longer takes the last listed hit. Among windowed hits the latest `valid_from` wins (a missing start is the oldest); if the remaining candidates (equally late windows, or several unwindowed rates) carry different rates it raises `CalcError("ambiguous metal index rates ...")`. Windows are inclusive whole days against a scenario timestamp.
4. **Rule 4 conditions**: `valid_condition` (line 213-219) uses `_not_later` on both inclusive endpoints; a scenario at 23:30Z on the `valid_to` day is still inside, and it no longer raises `TypeError` when the scenario is a `datetime` (previously `date &lt;= datetime` would have crashed once a timestamp was passed).

### Tests added in `agent/tests/test_calc.py` (invented values: `T-0001`, `M-100`, `IDX`, `example.com`)

- `Quantity.test_same_day_change_after_scenario_time_ignored`, `test_same_day_change_with_offset`, `test_date_only_change_on_scenario_day_applies`, `test_future_withdrawal_does_not_apply`
- `Requirement.test_amendment_after_scenario_time_ignored`
- `Pricing.test_condition_valid_on_timestamp`, `test_scenario_timestamp_in_bid` (end-to-end `calculate()` with `scenario_date="2026-05-15T09:00:00Z"`)
- `Discount.test_record_after_scenario_time_is_future` (rule 21 helper with UTC, offset and date-only records)
- `MetalIndex.test_overlapping_windows_latest_start_wins`, `test_ambiguous_windows_raise`, plus an inclusive-end-day assertion in `test_window_contains_date`

One design note for the caller: a naive `datetime` from a site is treated as UTC. If a worker ever extracts local wall-clock times without an offset, it should attach the offset before handing them to calc.</result>

<result>The role mapping survives a mutated run: in all 4 runs, `briefing.py` mapped the bid and mail sites to the renamed ids. The worker checks are weaker: the tender read passed 3 of 3 final runs, but the mail lookup passed only 1 of 3, and both fail for reasons other than the mutation. ## Robustness results
Each run renames the site ids to site-a..d (shuffled) and replaces the product names. The model rewords the intro and submission paragraphs with every value kept, and the sections and bullets are reordered. A second variant also hides the URLs from the briefing step. Final script, seeds 2–4:

| Check | Seed 2 | Seed 3 | Seed 4 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Briefing, renamed ids (roles, reference, company, VAT id) | PASS 27s | PASS 86s | PASS 79s |
| Briefing, masked URLs (roles from the brief text alone) | PASS 17s | PASS 22s | PASS 51s |
| Mail address (`Mailbox.address`) | PASS 4s | PASS 2s | PASS 4s |
| Mail lookup, no TAN (director approval) | PASS 70s | FAIL 75s | FAIL 74s |
| Tender positions (5, same each run) | PASS 89s | PASS 44s | PASS 77s |
| Read-only guard | 0 blocked | 0 blocked | 0 blocked |

Whole runs took 139–200s.

- **Mail lookup failures:** both ran out of the 20-step budget. The mailbox needs a narrowed search behind gates that lock again on every page. Workers only see the siteId as a label, so this is a capability gap, not a mutation effect.
- **Seed 1** ran with vaguer goals: both briefing variants passed, while the mail lookup and tender read ran out of steps.
- **A hang:** in one attempt, starting the mail session hung for over 10 minutes after the cookie click timed out. `agent.solve()` starts the sessions with no time limit, so the same hang would block the real run.

## Audit, highest risk first
**HIGH**
1. `research_mail.py:210, 377-382, 448, 525`: a sender counts only if it is exactly a role name from the brief at the bidder's own mail domain. A brief that names people or outside addresses rejects every record without saying so. Accept the sender identity the brief states.
2. `research_mail.py:270-273`: the prompt states public-1's search behaviour as fact (AND search, nothing above 20 hits, separate archive search), which is close to a fixed click path. Say "search every folder; narrow the search if nothing shows".
3. `research_tender.py:263-272` and `research_mail.py:181-224`: which facts live on which site is fixed in code. Map facts to sites from the brief through a model.
4. `research_mail.py:283` and `research_tender.py:81-84`: the model converts numbers itself ("5,15 → 5.15"), so en-US "1,250" becomes 1.25. Keep the raw strings and parse them with `evidence.parse_number`.

**MED**
- The `research.py` goals describe public-1 traps: "Verbindliche Unterlagen", Originalwortlaut, one record per page, "search help".
- `evidence.py` defaults to German formats: a lone comma is a decimal, slashed dates are day-first, and the "none" words are German.
- `submit.py:52-53`: TAN messages assumed "newest first, highest request number".
- `submit.py:86`: "German number format" is hardcoded. Use the format the brief's submission steps name.
- `briefing.py` assumes exactly one bid site and one mail site, and ignores `submission.siteId` from the run contract.
- `browser_tools.py:85-91, 173-194`: the sign-in, cookie and error-page helpers only know German and English wording and one-step password forms.
- `calc.py:333-336`: the same sender-at-bidder-domain assumption as finding 1.

**LOW:** the German hints in prompts (`worker.py:21` "German industrial company", USt-IdNr., Konditionsgruppe), rule numbers in comments, the `## Site access` split in `research.py:152`, and `dev/smoke.py` goals keyed on public-1 site ids.

## Recommended fixes
- Give `solve()` a time limit on session start, with one retry.
- Use `task["submission"]["siteId"]` to confirm the bid site the briefing returns.
- Make mail lookups take the search hints from the brief, and give them a budget that allows for gates locking again.
- Parse every number through one locale-aware parser.
- Treat sender rules and fact locations as data read from the brief, not code.

<summary>Agent "Cut tokens in page views" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I've committed the view changes on branch `worktree-agent-aca9e958470992e7f`, rebased onto current main. All 73 tests pass. The biggest finding was a bug rather than a token saving: the old history trim removed the page the model was on. Token savings are clear on form pages (about 25% fewer input tokens) and small on read-only browsing.

**What changed**
- **Latest view kept (bug fix, `worker.py`).** The old trim kept the page only in the newest request. So after any `note()` call the model had no page in front of it and invented values. Traces show refused notes with made-up dates and prices, and pages reopened over and over. Now the newest page view stays until a newer one arrives. The first prompt's page view is cut to its URL once a newer view exists.
- **Same-page diffs (`extract.py`, `browser_tools.py`).** After click, fill, select, check, goto or back on the same page, the model gets only the changed parts, or "unchanged". Each control counts as its own piece, so a changed field is one short line, and refs in unchanged parts stay valid. Navigating to another page, or a change bigger than 40% of the page, still sends the whole view. The full view stays in context, so it stays in the cached prefix.
- **Long selects.** Option lists over 600 characters show the count, the first 8 and the last 2. A new `options(ref, contains)` tool lists or searches them. A failed `select` now names close matches instead of waiting 10 s for a timeout. Option text the model looks up counts as seen, so the grounding check still covers it.
- **Grounding check unchanged in behaviour.** `session.seen` still gets every whole view.
- **Stopped workers now report usage.** Workers that hit the step limit used to log no token counts; now they record requests, input, cached and output tokens.
- **Prompt caching** needed no change: the instructions and tools never vary between steps.

**Main moved, one fix needed.** The new `agent/research.py` reads `session.look()` text directly to extract content. So diffs only come from the browser tools (`look(brief=True)`). Every other caller still gets the whole view, and such a read resets the diff baseline. I did not touch `research.py`.

**Before/after**

Read-only smoke goals, 3 runs each. All runs used the same scouting account, alternating base and new:

| site | variant | done | requests | input tokens | cached | seconds | tokens/request |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tender-portal | base | 0/3 | 26.3 | 117,915 | 68,469 | 134 | 4,478 |
| tender-portal | new | 1/3 | 24.0 | 94,524 | 55,403 | 119 | 3,938 |
| customer-portal | base | 3/3 | 26.7 | 97,077 | 56,971 | 126 | 3,640 |
| customer-portal | new | 2/3 | 26.3 | 88,180 | 51,221 | 105 | 3,349 |
| erp | base | 3/3 | 6.7 | 16,199 | 6,795 | 23 | 2,430 |
| erp | new | 3/3 | 7.3 | 18,391 | 9,931 | 33 | 2,508 |
| mail | base | 3/3 | 2.7 | 8,584 | 1,045 | 24 | 3,219 |
| mail | new | 3/3 | 6.3 | 18,848 | 9,408 | 42 | 2,976 |

- **Mail costs more, but the answers are correct.** The inbox shows 3 messages per page. Base returned "5 newest" after 1 to 3 requests by guessing items 4 and 5, or saying it couldn't see them. New opens page 2 and returns the right five.
- **Model wandering dominates the noise.** The model often keeps going after it has the answer, so read-only results vary a lot between runs.
- **Diffs barely fired on read-only sites**, since those runs mostly navigate between pages.

To test diffs where they matter, I made an invented local form (fill quantity and reference, pick 1 of 61 options, tick terms, save). This proxies the bid configurator, which I never opened:

| variant | runs | correct | requests | input tokens | seconds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| base | 7 | 7/7 | 6 to 7 | ~27,700 | 19 to 28 |
| new | 13 | 13/13 | 7 to 8 | 17,700 to 21,900 | 20 to 38 |

The configurator spends most of its steps on same-page actions, so this is where the 217k-token bid worker should save most. That is an estimate; I could not measure it under the read-only rule.

**Evaluated and dropped**
- **Repeated boilerplate removal.** Limited to lines that can't hide content, it saved only 1 to 4% of view text. Without that limit it would hide status words that are traps ("gültig", "ersetzt", table headers).
- **Path-only URLs in old history stubs.** Tiny gain, and that part is mostly cached anyway.

Per request, about 5k characters are fixed instructions and tools, about 3k history and 1.5 to 2.5k the current view. 40 to 55% of input tokens were reported as cached. Browser actions take 0.3 to 0.7 s and a model step 1 to 4 s, so the number of steps drives both tokens and time.

**Accuracy risks**
- The model must combine a diff with its last full view. On the form it did so correctly every time (20/20 runs), but the real configurator hasn't been tested.
- With a long select, the model sometimes calls `options()` even when it knows the exact text, which costs one extra request. It never picks an option automatically, so look-alike entries like "&lt;ref&gt;-A" can't be chosen by mistake.
- If the model never calls `options()`, it can't see the hidden options in a long list.

**Branch and commits**
- `660d2ec` Trim page views and keep the latest one (includes the `docs/learned.md` lessons)
- `622a81e` Send view diffs only from browser tools

Not pushed or merged.

My measurement scripts are outside the repo, in `/tmp/rfqbench/`. `bench.py` is a smoke variant whose tender goal forbids the bid area, and `formbench.py` is the invented form. Two run-time notes:
- One early run hung for over 15 minutes during site start-up and did not happen again. The bench now has a timeout there; `SiteSession.start()` in the agent still has none.
- One ERP run hit the site's "guest_unavailable" error.</result>

**Why mail is slow and unreliable:**
- **Proxy latency:** model calls were taking 30 to 36 s, so they hit the 30 s client timeout and retried. Several kinds made only 1 or 2 model calls in their 150 s budget, and the planner alone took about 230 s in that run.
- **Navigation model:** with thinking off, Qwen invented message ids and subjects and lost track of which folder it was in. `keep_results` now only opens links the worker has actually seen. With thinking on it is more reliable but too slow for 20 steps.
- **Site:** actions on the mail site sometimes stall for 30 s.
- **Fragile reading:** the director's approval needs a subject the brief never names, so it depends on the planner's guess (it usually picks Rabattfreigabe, but has used other words that find nothing).

**Harness changes I recommend:**
1. Give `back()` a `reason: str = ""` parameter, as `submit.py`'s tools have. Qwen calls it with arguments, its retries run out and the worker stops.
2. Add a timeout parameter to `llm.model()`. Thinking readers need about 180 s, so I built `reader_model()` with `with_options`.
3. Let `run_worker` drop `note()` and the "record every fact with note()" instruction. Refused notes waste a lot of steps.
4. Cap `SiteSession.settle()`/`act()` time: a cookie click that never returned once hung a tab for minutes, and form presses take 30 s.
5. Read nested optional objects tolerantly: Qwen sends them as JSON strings. `MailRecord` decodes them; other typed outputs may need the same.
6. Mail search: the planned `site_tools.py` search tool should take my `collect_pages` and `keep_results` helpers. My view is that mail search won't hit the time target until searches cost one step each.
7. calc takes only one setup pool and one bonus plan; `bid_clauses` reports a problem if there are several.

The tender records feed calc directly (quantity changes, withdrawals, amendments). End reserves are returned per key with their revision for the spec matcher.</result>

<summary>Agent "Finish site tools measurement (Opus)" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>The site tools work is finished in the worktree and rebased onto the latest main (d9ee047), with 5 commits on the branch. All 95 tests pass. With the tools, two of three read-only tasks came back correct; without them, none did. The measurement is uneven, though, because the mail site was gating and hanging logins during the runs (details below). Nothing was pushed or merged.

**What each tool does** (`agent/site_tools.py`; each acts only on what the model points at)
- **`search(field_ref, query, submit_ref="")`**: types the model's query into the model's field, submits, and returns the numbered result rows over every next or load-more page. Each row comes with its link refs and targets. It also passes on what the site says about the result set ("N Treffer", "Seite X von Y", too many hits, no hits).
- **`collect(list_ref="page", next_ref="")`**: pages through the list the model points at, or the page's main list, and returns each row once. It stops when no new row shows, with a cap of 25 pages or 200 rows.
- **`extract(fields)`**: one model call (thinking off) over the current view. Each field comes back with its value as written and the exact quote it came from. A field whose quote is not on the page is dropped, and numbers and dates are annotated with their possible readings.
- **Number, price and date readers**: these settle locale and day/month order only when the text does; otherwise they return every reading.
- All controls are found by role and position, never by site labels, URLs or values. Every page is read the normal way, so the grounding check sees each row.

**Measured** (local, scouting sites, read-only, no bid form, no TANs)

| Task | Variant | Correct answer | Steps | Seconds | Input tokens |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ERP search the site refuses as too broad | with tools | yes, "too many hits" reported | 10 | 46 | 34k |
| same | plain | no (step limit) | 30 | 153 | 112k |
| Mailbox search, inbox and archive | with tools | yes, 10 messages, newest right | 18 | 105 | 93k |
| same | plain | no (step limit in run 1; hung on the site's gates in run 4) | 30 / 3 | 137 / 277 | not recorded / 9k |
| Spec revision list | both | no (step limit) | 30 | 126 to 135 | not recorded |

- The mail comparison is not clean, so treat it as "tools succeeded, plain failed" rather than a ratio.
- In the spec task, `collect` read the 4-page document register in one call. The spec document was not in it, which is a problem with the task wording, not the tool.
- ERP search the site accepts: not measured (I had no query I knew would list hits).
- One combined run had a worker stuck at sign-in for 14 minutes. Sign-in (`session.start`) has no time limit. That is a harness issue, not part of this branch.

**Two bugs found and fixed after rebasing** (new commit 892e098)
- **The collected list disappeared.** `worker.py` cut every older tool result to one line as soon as a newer page view arrived. The model then paged back by hand to see the list it had just collected. Results of search, collect and extract now stay in the history, up to 12k characters.
- **The next page came as a diff the model could not use.** Main's new diff-only views would have shown the model changes against a page it never saw, since `paginate` clicks through pages without showing them. `paginate` now resets that baseline, so the next view comes whole.
- The list output also now says its refs hold only until the next action.

**Fair play (rule 11)**
- **search**: the model picks the site, field, query and when to search; the tool only fills, submits and pages through that one result set. This is the "paginate a model-selected result set" case.
- **collect**: paginates only the list the model points at and opens no rows. Which rows matter, and what to open next, stays with the model, so it is not a crawler.
- **extract**: parses a page the model chose, for fields the model names. Values must quote the page and nothing is computed or decided.
- **Readers**: deterministic calculation and validation. They return every reading instead of guessing.
- No site values are in code, tests or prompts. The test pages are invented, and the check script takes its query words on the command line.

**For the coordinator (mail research)**: yes, `search` and `collect` can cover the mail worker's paging. Every page they click through is added to the text `keep_results` already checks for seen links, so `keep_results` still only opens links the worker actually saw.
- **What changes in `research_mail.py`**: in `research_kind`, pass `tools=(search, collect, keep_results_tool(result.kept))` instead of `paging_tool(Collected())`.
- **Goal wording**: per folder, call `search(field_ref, "&lt;reference&gt; &lt;subject word&gt;")` once; it returns every hit over all pages in one step. Then call `keep_results` with the exact subjects. For a list already on screen, use `collect("page")`.
- **Cost per search**: one step instead of three (fill, submit, `collect_pages`). The return is compact rows rather than up to 30k characters of raw page views.
- **Tender Q&amp;A**: keep `collect_pages` there for now, because its reader needs the whole page views, which `collect` does not keep.

**Commits on `worktree-agent-aab59b204c79e220a`**: 5ecdb61 → 0aec6ae (search, collect, extract), 8fd6e38 → 0d4d2bc (extract usage, single-row lists), e832ed4 → 4e90bf6 (list controls after the list only), a8e8c08 → 974a897 (pagination inside nav, counts), and the new 892e098 "Keep list tool results; whole view after paging", which also adds the `docs/learned.md` bullet. All are authored by josteng. The Entire-Checkpoint lines are kept, and nothing under `.claude/` is staged. I deleted the measurement traces from the worktree.

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Finish model timeouts (Opus)" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I've finished the task in the worktree and found a real bug along the way: the per-mode timeouts the previous agent added never fired on slow calls. The test it was fixing was already correct in commit 55fc457. All 108 tests pass after the rebase, and there's one commit, `90c0cc4` "Use per-mode model timeouts with retry logging", on branch `worktree-agent-a78d2a106642d0cc6`. Nothing was pushed or merged.

**The bug.** When the model takes longer than about 35 s, the proxy sends the response headers and then a newline every ~15 s until it finishes or gives up at 300 s. The client's read timeout only measures the gap between chunks, so it never fired. In a raw curl check, one thinking-on call ran 300 s and received only 18 newline bytes. With the commit as it was, a thinking-on call hung for over 275 s with no retry.

**What changed:**
- **`agent/llm.py`:**
  - **Whole-call deadline:** `model()` now uses a small HTTP client subclass that turns the per-request timeout into a deadline for the whole call. When it expires it raises `ReadTimeout`, and the OpenAI client then retries. The call log marks cut-off calls as `incomplete`.
  - **Defaults:** thinking-off is now 45 s instead of 30 s. Thinking-on stays at 90 s.
  - **Retries:** `LLM_RETRIES=0` now turns retries off.
  - **`env_seconds()`:** the old `_positive()` is now public.
- **`agent/research.py`:** `_slow_model()` set `LLM_TIMEOUT` temporarily to get a longer timeout on the client. The per-request timeout from `settings()` overrides that, so it didn't work. It's replaced by `_read_settings(thinking)`, which passes `LLM_READ_TIMEOUT` (default 90 s) through `llm.settings(..., timeout=...)`.
- **`agent/tests/test_llm.py`:** three new tests check that a slow trickling body times out, that late headers time out, and that a normal call is read in full.

**Measured against the proxy on 127.0.0.1:8765.** `atira scout --env` hung, so I read the proxy address from the proxy's log instead.

| Call | Duration | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Thinking-off, 12 at once | 2.3 to 4.3 s | 2 of the 12 hung until the proxy returned 502 at 300 s, which is the case the deadline now cuts off |
| Thinking-on, 8 at once | 26 to 41 s | All finished within the 90 s deadline with no retry |
| Short thinking-on | 6 to 8 s | No retry |
| Long thinking-on, 45 s deadline | 45 s | Cut off as expected, retried once, then failed |
| Thinking-off copying 80 table rows | ~85 s | Shows that time grows with the length of the answer, not only the mode |

I chose 45 s for thinking-off because steps take 2 to 5 s and it leaves room for the 30 to 36 s calls the mail agent saw. Calls that write long answers should pass their own timeout.

**For the mail and tender agents (please pass this on):** the `reader_model()` workaround in `research_tender.py`, which `research_mail.py` imports, no longer works on this branch. The per-request timeout from `llm.settings()` beats the client timeout set with `with_options(timeout=180)`, so those readers currently get 90 s. They should use `llm.model()` and pass the timeout per call:
```python
Agent(llm.model(), ..., model_settings=llm.settings(thinking=True, timeout=180))
```
Setting `LLM_TIMEOUT_THINKING` or `LLM_TIMEOUT` changes the default for every call in that mode. I left both files alone, as the coordinator asked.

**Rebase:** it applied cleanly onto the current main (now at `65f4aa9`). Main's `llm.thinking(phase, default)` and the `THINKING_*` switches had already been in the base, and the briefing and submit code still use them.

**Commit message:** reworded as you specified, keeping the `Entire-Checkpoint:` line. Only `agent/` files are in the commit; nothing under `.claude/` was staged.</result>

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "v2 configurator position filling" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Position filling for v2 is built on top of main's phased bid flow. On my offline mock of the bid wizard it enters, signs and submits a fully correct bid. Live on the scouting tender portal it filled a position correctly in an earlier run, but the most reliable version hasn't had a clean live run yet: my last attempts hung at startup, before reaching the bid form.

Branch `worktree-agent-a6463a61263f3c79d`, rebased on main `e05fb7f`, 6 commits, nothing pushed or merged. All 118 unit tests pass.

**Design**
- **Input:** `solve()` and `submit_bid()` now take the answer dict (`BidResult.answer()`) plus an `attributes` map: per position, the material's attributes (bore, stroke, mounting, certification). The answer dict has no attributes, but the cascading selects need them before the catalogue-number list appears. The research code on main already produces them (`evidence.py`'s `Requirement.attributes()`), so wiring is one dict per position.
- **Incomplete positions** (any of material, quantity, unit price or lead time missing) are omitted. Without an answer, every position is omitted, as in v1.
- **What the model decides:** in the form phase it says which position is on screen, maps each control label to a value name once, and names the confirm button and the next button.
- **What the helper does (`agent/configurator.py`, `configure_position()`):** it sets each cascade level and clicks the confirm, checks the choice survived the round trip, picks the catalogue number, fills German-format numbers, sets the lead time, then clicks the model-named next button. After every position it reports the running net total of the positions actually entered.
- **Failures:** if a position can't be filled, the tool says so and the model omits it; after 3 failures it refuses. Close to the deadline it tells the model to omit the rest. The signature is always reached.
- **Summary check and signing pass** now receive the expected values of each entered position, the "all others omitted" rule and the computed net total.
- No site names, labels, URLs or values are in `agent/`.

**Traps**

| Trap | Handling |
| --- | --- |
| Bogus extra position | The tool never clicks "add". It answers "no values, omit it" for any position not in the answer. The goal says never add; the summary check flags extra positions. |
| Shuffled order | The model reads the position number on the page; the tool checks it appears in the heading or above the first control before filling. |
| Text-sorted options | Matching is by value, never by list position: same number, or the catalogue number as a whole token, so a prefix like 120 never picks 1200. |
| Worded attributes | If an attribute is worded differently from the options ("none" vs "without certification"), the model may pick the option itself. Numbers must match exactly. |
| Alternative offer as default | The goal requires a main offer, or the no-bid declaration when the decision is no_bid; the summary check checks it. |
| Gross vs net | The model enters only the running net total the tool reports, never a gross amount, its own sum or a sum the page proposes. |

**Results**
- **Offline mock** (invented values, deliberately different labels): signed and fully correct in 64 to 92 s with about 13 model requests. That covers 2 positions entered, 1 incomplete one omitted, a worded-attribute fallback, a look-alike catalogue number, main offer, total 9390,70 and terms unticked.
- **Live on scouting** (always stopped before the TAN request):
  - **Earlier flow, thinking on:** one position fully entered via the fallback, a position with an impossible bore failed and was omitted, and net total 259,14 was correct. 202 s.
  - **Phased flow, thinking off:** several slips, each fixed:
    - The model typed its own total.
    - It clicked "omit" right after a successful fill, which is why the tool now presses the model-named next button.
    - A partial field map replaced the first one.
    - It picked bore 50 for an impossible 51.
    - Once, a re-login on the shared login reset the wizard to step 1.
  - **Latest version:** no clean live run. Three attempts hung before the bid form because one site's startup (the webmail) never finished inside `solve()`, while the sites answered curl instantly. That start step has no timeout and could use one.
- **Sandbox test run `run_e7312a942ed0a3e4`** (all positions omitted, before the phased flow): quality 10, eligible, 202 s, $0.021. A filled-position test run isn't possible until research and calc are wired into `solve()`.

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Streaming watchdog for hung model calls" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I added the watchdog behind `LLM_STREAM_WATCHDOG` (off by default) and committed it on branch `worktree-agent-a1d0cca4a7d0d81f7`; nothing is pushed or merged. The proxy does stream, but only text and thinking come token by token: tool-call arguments arrive all at once at the end. So hangs before the first chunk are caught in 20 s, but a hang inside a tool call can't be told apart from a long legitimate one. The test suite passes (117 tests). Hedging is implemented and unit-tested, but I didn't measure it live.

**1. Does the proxy stream?** (raw byte-level checks against 127.0.0.1:8765)
- **Text and reasoning:** they arrive token by token in both modes, with gaps under 1.5 s. Thinking deltas come as `reasoning_content`.
- **Tool calls:** the arguments are held back until they are complete. A 60-row output tool sent nothing for 64 s, then everything at once; with thinking on, reasoning streams and then the tool part goes quiet for up to 58 s. With `tool_choice=required`, which is what our agents' output tools use, even the tool name only comes at the end. Asking for more frequent usage stats doesn't help.
- **Keep-alives:** quiet stretches carry `: keepalive` comments every 10 s. The OpenAI SDK drops these, so they never count as progress.
- **First chunk:** the response headers arrive together with the first chunk, within 9.4 s in 179 of 180 calls (p90 5.9 s, either mode).
- **What a hang looked like:** I caught one hang in 180 calls (thinking off). It sent no headers and no bytes at all for 200 s, so with streaming a hang looks like "no first chunk".
- **No 300 s cutoff when streaming:** the proxy doesn't cut streamed answers. One runaway thinking-on answer ran 814 s and 32k tokens.

**2. Design** (`agent/llm.py`)
- `llm.model()` now returns a subclass of pydantic-ai's OpenAI model. With the switch on, it streams each call internally and hands back one normal response, so `run_worker`, briefing, submit and the readers run unchanged. With the switch off, behaviour is exactly as before.
- **First-chunk limit:** no first chunk within `LLM_FIRST_TOKEN_TIMEOUT` (20 s) means the call is dropped and retried.
- **Idle limit:** a pause in text or reasoning longer than `LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT` (15 s) is retried. This only applies to requests without tools. Requests with tools (almost all of ours) instead get their existing per-call timeout (45, 90 or 180 s) as allowed silence, counted from the last token rather than from the start of the call.
- **Overall cap:** `LLM_MAX_CALL` (300 s).
- **Retries:** up to `LLM_RETRIES`, with the same backoff as today; each one is logged as `[model] stalled ...`.
- **Hedging:** `LLM_HEDGE_AFTER` sends a second copy if the first hasn't started by then, keeps whichever starts first and cancels the other. The cancelled copy may still be counted in the simulated cost.
- **Tests:** `agent/tests/test_llm_watchdog.py` uses fake streamed answers: a normal stream, a call that never starts, a keep-alive trickle, a slow but progressing stream, thinking deltas, a held-back tool call, giving up after the retries, hedging, and the switch off.

**3. Measured** (the same 30 agent calls per mode, both modes run at the same time)

| Calls | Mode | Median | p90 | Max | Retries | Failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 22 calls (off short/tool/long, on long) | current deadlines | 14.4 s | 81 s | 268 s | several 90 s timeouts | 0 |
| | watchdog | 13.2 s | 74 s | 89 s | 2 (no first chunk, caught at 20 s) | 0 |
| 8 thinking-on calls with very long reasoning | current deadlines | 257 s | 511 s | 497 s | 23 (90 s timeouts) | 2 of 8 |
| | watchdog | 422 s | 832 s | 811 s | 6 (300 s cap) | 0 |

On the second group, the old deadline repeatedly killed answers that were still coming, failing 2 of 8. The watchdog finished all 8 but let the runaway answers run to 300 s each.

**4. Recommended defaults**
- **Settings:** turn on `LLM_STREAM_WATCHDOG=1` with first chunk 20 s and idle 15 s, but lower `LLM_MAX_CALL` to about 150 s. A safer alternative is to limit output tokens for thinking-on calls. As the bench shows, progress checks alone never stop runaway reasoning.
- **Hedging:** leave it off for now. Hangs are rare (about 0.6% here), the 20 s retry already handles them, and hedging can double the cost.
- **Next step:** A/B it with `dev/test-run.sh`; the `LLM_*` settings are recorded automatically.

**Commits**
- `7b2a144` Add opt-in stream watchdog for model calls
- `e75e8c9` Record stream and watchdog measurements (two bullets in `docs/learned.md`)

My worktree had been created from an older commit, so I first moved the branch to current main (`e05fb7f`, the 180 s reader deadline); it had no commits of its own. The probe and bench scripts are outside the repo in `/tmp/rfqprobe/`, along with a temporary private file holding the model keys (`/tmp/rfqprobe/qwen.env`), which you can delete.</result>

<summary>Agent "Find and fix the event-loop hang" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>I couldn't pin down the exact cause of the hang from the log. The fixes for every likely blocker are committed, along with a watchdog that will dump stacks if it happens again. The sandbox run of the branch did not hang, but it also failed (score 0) for a different reason, described below.

**Most likely cause.** "Nothing at all for 20+ min, and no deadline ever fired" fits a blocked event loop better than a hung await. The log shows nothing that blocks for that long, so the candidates are:
- **Frozen page plus unbounded calls.** I reproduced this locally: on a tab running an endless JS loop, `page.evaluate`, `frame.evaluate`, `page.title()` and `locator.count()` hang forever with no timeout. Other tabs keep working, and `asyncio.wait_for` does cut these calls. `observe()`, `look()`, `locate()`, `_human_check` and `_collect_closed` had no bound. `look()` is also called outside any `wait_for` in many places, including the first view in `run_worker`. The two `Locator.evaluate: Timeout 30000ms exceeded` errors on mail tabs, and the 45 s settle timeout on the ERP tab, show pages becoming unresponsive just before the silence. On its own this explains a stuck worker, not the missed deadlines.
- **Quadratic regex in the grounding check.** `unseen_values()` used `[\w/.:,+-]*\d[\w/.:,+-]*`, which is quadratic on any long run of those characters with no digit. Measured: 20k characters take 1.1 s, so 200k would take about 110 s and 1M about 45 min, all on the loop. It only runs on model output and notes, and the mail workers run with `grounded=False`, so it probably isn't what hung at 362 s.
- **Ruled out:**
  - The diff in `changes()` is only run on views of 14k characters or less, and stays around 10 ms on a worst-case table.
  - I found no `SiteSession.lock` re-entrance. Mailbox lock then session lock is always taken in that order, never reversed.
  - Nothing swallows `CancelledError`.
- **Infra possibility.** If the sandbox stopped reading stdout, `print(flush=True)` would block the whole loop and give exactly this picture. I can't verify that from our side.

**Fixes (commit `630849a`):**
- **Watchdog:** new `agent/loopwatch.py`, started in `agent.solve()`.
  - A daemon thread watches a 1 s heartbeat. If the loop is stale for more than 20 s, it prints a `[watchdog]` line and dumps every thread's stack to stdout with `faulthandler`, at most once a minute.
  - If the loop still runs but no trace event has appeared for 300 s, it dumps all asyncio task stacks. That catches the deadlock case too.
  - `tracing.py` now records the time of the latest event (two lines).
- **`extract.py`:** the page walk is capped at 20 s. Per-frame evaluates, `locate()`'s `count()` and `select_options` are capped at 5 to 20 s. `changes()` returns None (send the whole view) when the two views together exceed 40k characters.
- **`browser_tools.py`:**
  - The human-check and popup-collect frame evaluates have timeouts, and so does `page.title()`.
  - `look()` now returns "(the page does not respond …)" instead of hanging when its retry also fails.
  - `unseen_values()` uses a linear regex that gives the same results. It also searches `seen` directly instead of rebuilding a whitespace-normalised copy on every call; values contain no whitespace, so the result is the same.

**Tests.** The new `agent/tests/test_hang.py` checks that:
- a 250k-character diff and the grounding check (200k-character token against about 2 MB of history) each finish in under 1 s;
- a frozen page is cut off and `look()` returns a note, while another tab still works;
- the Python side of `look()` on a 100k+ character page takes under 1 s;
- the watchdog dumps on a blocked loop and on a silent run.

The full suite passes: 142 tests.

**Sandbox run `run_a27e8c91f74fdb41`:** failed with score 0.
- tender-portal, which is the bid site, never opened (repeated 45 s settle timeouts), so research had nothing to build on.
- There was no hang and no watchdog dump: the research deadline fired at 630 s as designed.
- The run then crashed with `IndexError` in `SiteSession.page` (`open_pages[-1]` on an empty list), reached from `look()` when `submit_bid()` started the bid worker on a session with no tab.
- That crash is an existing bug, and the fix belongs in `submit.py` or `agent.py`, which I left alone. Someone should make `submit_bid` reopen the bid site, or exit cleanly, when its session has no tab.
- The website's log stream timed out twice while I followed the run; I recorded the result with `dev/track.py --force`. `experiments/runs.jsonl` now has two lines for this run, an early "running" one and the final "failed" one.

**Branch:** `worktree-agent-a6e9ece93fe436042`, with commits `630849a` (the fixes) and `1374502` (the run record). The `Entire-Checkpoint` line is kept and there is no session trailer. Nothing was pushed or merged.</result>
