**Changes**
1. **Spec, two attempts at once** (`orchestrator.py` and the spec part of `research.py`):
   - A fast attempt (thinking off, 25 steps) and a careful one (thinking on, 35 steps) start together, each in its own browser context so they don't share a sign-in or an access choice.
   - The first attempt with a revision and requirements wins and the other is cancelled. Its context is closed straight away so it doesn't slow the ERP tabs.
   - Requirements go to the ERP as soon as either attempt reads them, all from one revision. If the winner picked a different revision, the ones already sent are replaced.
   - The spec worker's instructions are now generic: find the buyer's specification document, open its revision history, identify the revision marked current. It uses `collect` for the document list and the revision list.
   - If the spec worker stops early, the revisions are still read from the page it ended on.
   - Requirement pages it showed but didn't read in full still count, only if the page names the chosen revision and only for requirements not already read. Requirements read before an attempt stops are kept.
2. **Partial answers:**
   - With no complete position the agent still answers: decision, tender reference, the discount from the approval if it was read (a conditional approval with nothing to meet its threshold gives 0) and total 0.
   - `agent.py` falls back to that answer if the research step crashes.
   - With some positions complete, the threshold is judged on those positions, else 0.
3. **Tender, documents and Q&amp;A in parallel:** they are read in two tabs side by side. Positions with their original quantities go on the board as soon as they are read and are replaced once the Q&amp;A resolves them, so a deadline in between still leaves positions to bid.
4. **Deadline and budgets** (after your scope note):
   - ERP research that finished by the deadline is kept.
   - The final material choice runs per requirement, so one slow model call doesn't cost the others.
   - Step limits grow with the time left (new `steps_for()` in `worker.py`) for the spec and tender steps.
   - The overall research deadline is unchanged.
5. **Mail, orchestrator scheduling only** (worth telling the mail agent): the approval kind runs first.
   - A kind that ran out of its budget without reading anything gets one more try if the mail deadline allows. Results from both tries are merged.
   - Each kind's budget is the mail time left shared over the waiting kinds (120 to 240 s).
   - It only uses a `stopped` flag or a `seconds=` option on `research_kind` if `research_mail.py` offers them, so it works with main as it is.
   - `research_mail.py` itself is back to main.

**Left to the ERP agent:** I removed my changes to the `Erp` class and `reselect`. The useful idea from them: a requirement whose search finished but whose materials weren't all read by the deadline could keep its hits, marked unfinished, so a later choice can still find the exact match among the materials read. If no match is found the position is left out, never turned into a no_bid.

**Changes (all in `agent/research_mail.py`; `orchestrator.py` and `site_tools.py` untouched):**
- **Missing mailbox address:** the address lookup now reads every page the tab has shown, not only the current view, and tries 3 times. If it still finds nothing, records are authorised by the domain most kept messages were sent to (new `recipient` field), instead of all being dropped. This works with the orchestrator's existing `resolve_mail(..., domain="")` call.
- **Reads run alongside the search:** each kept message is read as soon as it is kept, while the worker checks other folders.
- **Shorter waits:** a search ends 60 s after its first hit. Stalled model calls are retried sooner: reads after 60 s (was 180 s), the planner after 30 s (was 45 s), the address lookup after 20 s.
- **Hung tabs:** a tab with no new page view for 45 s is closed and replaced, and the kind tries again on the fresh tab. This addresses the hung tabs you saw in `run_3d85ba8d0e7c8267`.
- **Session expiry:** a message whose tab was redirected to sign-in is opened once more. In the local run this had given the reader a sign-in page and an empty project-bonus record.
- **Budgets and retries:** a kind's time shrinks as the mail deadline nears, and it always returns before the deadline, so its records are no longer lost to cancellation. A kind that kept nothing gets one more try, with a hint.
- **Planner:** the approval is planned first. A misspelled subject (e.g. "ue" for "ü") is now corrected in place instead of costing a model retry.
- **Orchestrator hook (your request):** `research_kind(..., seconds=)` sets the kind's whole budget, and `KindResult.stopped` is True when it ran out of time with nothing kept. Main's orchestrator detects both, so I made no orchestrator change.

<agent-message from="a61a1dc566d4dd696">
Priority answer (sites never opened): the site was very slow but still alive, and our start sequence threw away each attempt that was making progress.

Evidence (a27e, 9371, 487200 all show the same pattern; no recordings exist, so this is from traces and platform inputs):
- Each attempt is goto (30 s domcontentloaded cap), then settle (45 s cap), then look(), all inside START_SECONDS=90. The first settle timeout always lands at exactly 75.2 s (30+45), and start_failed fires at 90.0 / 180.0 / 270 / 360. So the navigation never fired domcontentloaded within 30 s, and no helper (login, cookies, human_check) found anything for 45 s.
- On attempt 1 browser 1 (tender) records ZERO inputs, so the login form never rendered. The page was not a chrome-error page: no ConnectionError and no reload of an error page; the errors are bare TimeoutError.
- The sites were intermittently alive. In 487200 the tender login page did render on attempt 2 after 5.8 s (login typed at 95.8 s), but the post-login page then hung: settle timed out at 135 s and look() timed out at 180 s. In a27e the tender login rendered on the 5th attempt (372.7 s) and the post-login page hung again (settle timeouts at 412 and 532 s, start_failed at 457 and 547 s).
- In 487200 the tender portal (browser 1) and ERP (browser 3) failed in lockstep with identical timestamps (75.2 / 90.0 / 180.0 / 504 / 594 s), while the customer portal and webmail in browsers 2 and 4 worked. Two different browsers stalling together points at the server side of those two sites, not one broken browser or our event loop. a27e had the loop watchdog and it never dumped.
- In other runs the same sites showed 'Locator.evaluate: Timeout 30000ms' on clicks (f93e, 164e, 3d85). That means the page's renderer or backend was stalling mid-run too.
- Collateral damage: the briefing waits for every start to finish (180 s) and the planner waits for a second pair of attempts (another 180 s). That is 360 s with nothing running (platform profile: 05:54 idle, 00:08 to 06:03, in a27e and 9371). Then an IndexError in look() on the tab-less session killed both a27e and 9371 (exit 1, 0 points; fixed later with revive).
- ERP alone failed to start in f93e (276 and 366 s), and all 5 positions were lost.

What would work better:
1) Never close a tab that is still loading. One attempt should keep waiting on the same tab with a longer cap (about 180 to 240 s). Today each retry throws away a navigation or login that was halfway through.
2) Keep retrying in the background with backoff (for example 30, 60, 120 s) until a hard deadline about 300 s before the end, instead of 2 attempts. Only the bid site truly gates points: a signed bid with no positions is worth 10, versus 0 without one.
3) Decouple the briefing and planner from site starts. The brief does not need the tender portal; start research on the sites that are up, and let the bid-site session keep warming in parallel. Starting the bid site first and keeping it warm is good, but it must not block anything.
4) A fresh browser context per attempt is unlikely to help, because two browsers failed in lockstep. A cheap way to rule it out is one attempt in a new context after two failures.
5) Make the start budget phase-aware: goto 60 s, then settle as long as the start budget allows, rather than fixed 30+45 inside 90.
</agent-message>

- **No queue pressure:** team-wide calls in flight peaked at 20, under the 32-per-team cap. The scored run shows 7 % of call time as queue wait and test runs 63–92 %, but total latency is the same, so that is how the platform counts time, not extra delay.
- **Site slowness:**
  - Settle timeouts and failed starts appear only in failing runs (2 to 8 each, none in the 100 runs).
  - Mail kinds hit their 150 s cap 2 to 10 times per run, including the 100 runs. Mean time per kind is 103–158 s and the max is 344 s.
  - The ERP 12-step limit was hit in 5 runs.

**3. Failure taxonomy** (16 runs, ranked by points lost)
1. **A site won't open or a page stalls:** 7 runs, about 690 points.
2. **Spec navigation fails or is too slow:** df14, 3d85, f910; about 270 points.
3. **False summary-check alarm, then signing goes back and times out:** f910, 3004; 200 points. Your c5b0 finding ("5 %" vs "5,00") makes it 3 runs.
4. **Research deadline cuts a slow worker:** 817c, 90 points.

**Critical path in the 100 runs:**
- The last research inputs are the mail kinds. end_reserves and condition_rules finished at 539–570 s; verify then took 17–45 s.
- Two requirements, covering 3 of the 5 positions, wait on the end_reserves mail.
- Form took 132–185 s; signing took 25–30 s, or 173 s with a bad TAN.
- The 100 runs were not faster than the failures. They had every site open on the first try, the spec found on the first attempt (or by the parallel spec attempts in 8e5c and 4b941), tender facts in before ~450 s, and a summary check that matched.

**4. Recommendations**
- **Summary check:** never let a mismatch switch signing to thinking or go back in the wizard. Sign as entered. Worth +200 points in 2 of 16 runs.
- **Spec:** run two parallel attempts from the start, each capped at about 150 s / 25 steps with a loop guard. The spec must be done by about 350 s so the ERP (100–130 s) and verify (cap 45 s; 3d85 took 137 s) fit.
- **Research deadline:** move it from 630 s to about 720 s. With the end at 1110 s, a 200 s form budget (seen max 185 s) and a 180 s signing reserve, research can run to 730 s. That would have saved 817c and probably 3d85. Put last_chance at the end minus 120 s.
- **Step limits:**
  - ERP material views: raise from 12 to 16–18, or scale with the time left.
  - Spec: keep 25 plus the loop guard.
  - Mail: give end_reserves and condition_rules 200 s and start them first; 150 s for the rest.
- **Where parallelism pays:** on the spec and on starting the critical mail kinds first, not on model calls.
- **Logging:** write the full trace to a file and print a short phase summary at the end, so the platform's line limit stops hiding the early phases.

<result>Slow website starts now keep waiting on the same tab instead of being thrown away, and nothing else waits for them. All 162 unit tests pass. The sandbox run reached quality 56 (eligible), but signing failed for a reason that has nothing to do with starts: a model 401 error.

**Branch:** `worktree-agent-aae38136b82fe0808` (based on d15f2e7, which includes 522dddc). Nothing pushed or merged.
- `c4f88be` Keep slow website starts alive on their tab
- `2de78fc` Test starts against a slow local site

## Design
**`agent/browser_tools.py` (`SiteSession`)**
- **Same tab:** one attempt has a 210 s budget (`SESSION_START_SECONDS`). It runs goto for up to 60 s and does not abort the request if that times out. It then loops settle (45 s cap) and look (30 s cap) on the same tab until the page shows something. Only a closed tab, the browser's own error page or the budget ends an attempt. If the budget runs out while the page shows something (e.g. a sign-in still pending), it returns that page rather than failing.
- **Pending sign-in:** if the sign-in form is still showing within 45 s of sending it on the same URL, the helper does not send it again; the start keeps waiting. Before, sending it again could cancel the post-login page that was still loading.
- **Retries:** after a failed attempt it waits 30, 60, then 120 s (repeating) before trying again. It keeps going until `until`; by default that is 300 s before the run's deadline (`browser_tools.DEADLINE`, set by `agent.solve`). `until` can be changed while a start is running.
- **Fresh context:** the third attempt runs in a new browser context. If that also fails, the session goes back to the original context and closes the new one.
- **Pending starts:** `revive()` waits for a start that is still running in another task instead of opening a second tab. So a session can be handed to a worker or to `submit_bid` before it is up.
- `_goto` now allows 60 s instead of 30 s.

**`agent/agent.py`**
- A new `warm_up()` starts all sign-ins as background tasks and returns at once, so the brief model call no longer waits for them.
- The bid site from the run contract keeps warming until 300 s before the deadline. Other sites give up after 600 s; the mail site is moved up to the long limit once the brief names it.
- The second pair of start attempts before research is gone. A trace event `starts state` records each site's state when research ends.

**`agent/orchestrator.py`**
- The orchestrator receives the warm sessions. A new `site_up(site)` makes each worker on a site (tender, qa, spec, mail tabs) wait for that site's warm session, then join, while workers on sites that are already up carry on.
- Worker starts retry until the research deadline.
- ERP tabs that `research.py` opens itself don't wait for their site; they only get the longer default retries.

## Tests
New file `agent/tests/test_slow_start.py`: 6 tests against a local `http.server` that stalls. The stalls (8 s) and limits (2–3 s per step) are about ten times shorter than real ones; I didn't add a full-scale 40–70 s variant.
1. A stalled home page loads on the same tab: one GET, no `start_failed`.
2. With a per-attempt cap shorter than the stall (the old setup), both attempts fail and the server sees two requests.
3. The sign-in page is fast but the post-login page stalls: the start waits, there is exactly one POST, and it ends on the dashboard.
4. A session handed out while its start is running: `look()` waits for it, with no second tab.
5. Nothing listening: three failed attempts, with the fresh context tried after two, then back to the original context.
6. `warm_up` returns in under 0.5 s and the brief stand-in finishes in under 2 s. A worker on a fast site joins in under 6 s, and one on the slow site joins when it comes up.

## Sandbox run `run_f7b4e2341bfdb1e1` (at commit c4f88be)
The platform only kept logs from about 150 s and from 465 s on; this is from what it kept.
- **Starts:**
  - The tender portal was slow under load again: at 164.8 s its warm session was still waiting on the same tab (`start_wait phase='settle'` on `/consent`).
  - No `start_failed` appears in the logs that were kept, and the tender and qa workers were working by 252 s.
  - ERP tabs started in 0.7–2.3 s.
  - At 706.8 s all four sites were up and no starts were pending.
- **Nothing blocked:** the orchestrator started at about 97 s (706.8 − 609.4). In the earlier runs the briefing finished at 180 s and the plan at 360–414 s.
- **Outcome:** the bid form was filled up to the signing step (4 positions, summary check OK at 826.7 s). Then the model started returning `401 authentication_error` at 851.8 s, so signing never happened. That looks like the run's model key being revoked near the time limit. One related observation: research took 609 s and only finished at 706.8 s, which left signing very close to that limit.

## Own-key facts from the docs

- **Allowed, through the proxy only.** fair-play.md: "any provider and model with your own key, through the proxy". Rejected: "reaching any model other than through the proxy".
- **Base URL.** run-contract.md: "`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` | `$LLM_PROXY_URL/anthropic`" is set in every run, and "Anthropic's [SDK] reads `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and sends `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as `x-api-key`". The proxy "forwards it unchanged and never stores, logs or returns it."
- **Network.** faq.md: "The `HTTP(S)_PROXY` variables in the run's environment route there; Python's … `httpx`, the Anthropic SDK … pick them up automatically."
- **Key as a team secret.** "For test and scored runs, keep it as a team secret, not in your package: we store packages and the review agent reads them." Rules:
  - names are "uppercase letters, digits and underscores … cannot be platform variables (`*_BASE_URL`, …)"
  - "at most 20; each value is 16 characters to 4 KB long"
  - "Setup never receives them."
  - There is no `atira` CLI command for secrets: use the team page or `PUT /v1/teams/me/secrets/&lt;NAME&gt;`.
- **Metering.** "Reported usage, including cached input and thinking tokens, is priced at each provider's published per-token prices", "There is no spending limit", and "unpriced models and models outside a configured allowlist are refused before forwarding". I probed the proxy with a fake key: `claude-haiku-5-5` and `claude-sonnet-5-5` were both forwarded (Anthropic answered "invalid x-api-key"), while a made-up model id was refused by the proxy itself.
- **`agent.json` `models`** lists hosted models only ("the run waits for those alone"). Own-key models are not listed there.
- **Final run.** scoring.md and cli.md: "Calls using your own model keys can cost up to **3×**" with Best of 3. So own keys are allowed in the private final.

## What changed

- **`agent/llm.py`**:
  - **Switch:** `LLM_BACKEND=anthropic` (default `qwen`) and `LLM_MODEL` (default `claude-haiku-5-5`). Without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` the run prints a warning and falls back to Qwen.
  - **Routing:** calls use `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, else `$LLM_PROXY_URL/anthropic`, else the Anthropic API directly (local only). The existing deadline client is reused, so `HTTP(S)_PROXY`, the per-mode timeouts, retries and the `[model]` call log all still apply.
  - **User-Agent:** calls send `error404ai-rfq-agent`.
- **Thinking mapping:**
  - **On:** adaptive thinking at `LLM_EFFORT` (default `medium`), with `drop_block`. Without it, our worker shrinking older page views would get 400s on the 5.5 models.
  - **Off:** thinking disabled on Haiku 5.5; `between_tools` on Sonnet 5.5, which rejects "disabled"; adaptive at low effort on Opus 5.5, which cannot turn thinking off. With thinking off, old thinking blocks are removed from the history before each call.
  - **Temperature** is not sent: the 5.5 models reject it.
- **Caching and structured output:**
  - Cache breakpoints sit on the instructions, the tools and the last two messages, so the prefix still hits after a step shrinks the previous page view.
  - Typed output works with the same agents; a fake Messages API test covers both thinking modes.
- **Stream watchdog:** stays Qwen-only. With Anthropic it prints a note and the whole-call deadline applies.
- **Dependencies:** `agent/pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` now pin `anthropic==1.13.0` and use `pydantic-ai-slim[anthropic,openai]==2.55.0`. They install under Python 3.12.13.
- **`.env` support:**
  - `agent/localenv.py` loads `agent/.env` for local runs only (skipped when `CHALLENGE_RUN_TOKEN` is set). Variables already in the environment win, and a missing file is fine.
  - `agent/.gitignore` already ignored `.env` but also ignored `.env.*`, so I added `!.env.example`.
  - Checked: `atira test-run ./agent --dry-run` lists `.env (.gitignore)` as excluded, and `git archive HEAD agent` contains only `.env.example`.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_llm_anthropic.py` and `tests/test_localenv.py`. The existing LLM tests now clear `LLM_BACKEND`, so a local `.env` cannot change their results. `agent/README.md` documents the new variables.

## Cost and speed estimate

Token counts come from `run_llm_calls` on our three quality-100 runs:
- input: 1.13M, 1.47M and 1.54M tokens (uncached plus cached; about 45–55 % cached on Qwen)
- output: 49k, 56k and 82k tokens
- simulated Qwen cost: $0.110, $0.141 and $0.191

Prices: Haiku 5.5 is $0.10 / $0.50 per million tokens (input / output) for prompts up to 100K tokens, which ours are; cache reads $0.01, cache writes $0.125. Sonnet 5.5 is $2 / $10; cache reads $0.20, cache writes $2.50. The ranges below allow for about 30 % more tokens with Claude's tokenizer.

| Per run (≈1.47M in, 56k out) | No caching | ~50 % cache hits | Range |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Qwen (measured, simulated price) | | | $0.11–0.19 |
| Haiku 5.5 | $0.175 | $0.127 | **$0.13–0.23** |
| Sonnet 5.5 | $3.50 | $2.55 | **$2.5–4.5** |

- **Speed:** on Qwen, about 75 % of model-call time is the hosted queue (one run: 22:27 waiting out of 29:50 in calls). Claude has no such queue, so runs should get shorter, but I could not measure this without a key.
- **Ranking:** cost × time only breaks ties after quality, and cost counts at least $0.01. Haiku is roughly level with Qwen if runs get faster. Sonnet costs about 20× more per run, which only pays off if it raises quality.
- **Billing:** Best of 3 can triple own-key spend in the final.
- **Rate limits:** we have up to 19 calls at once. Our Anthropic rate tier could return 429s; those are retried, and cache reads do not count toward the input limit.

## Rule risks and decisions for you

- **No fair-play problem.** Own keys through the proxy are explicitly allowed, and the key never enters the package.
- **The switch has to live in the package.** A team secret cannot hold `LLM_BACKEND=anthropic` because secret values must be at least 16 characters. To use Claude in sandbox runs, change the `agent.json` run command to `["env", "LLM_BACKEND=anthropic", "LLM_MODEL=claude-haiku-5-5", "uv", "run", "--no-sync", "python", "agent.py"]`. I would keep `"models": ["qwen"]` so the Qwen fallback stays warm. Neither change is committed: it changes what the next submission runs, so it is the team's call.
- **Untested against the real API.** The agents' prompts were tuned on Qwen, so quality on Haiku has to be checked with test runs before any submit.

<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>The ERP research on branch `worktree-agent-a3110b38964efe0ac` scored **quality 100** in its sandbox test run (`run_ff6a98847ab46e6e`), and the ERP phase finished in about 200 s. The branch is rebased on main `ffef7a8`, all unit tests pass, and nothing is pushed. One A-2 search still ran to its 16-step limit in that 100 run (details below).

**What changed (ERP part of `agent/research.py` only, plus a new test file)**
- **Group search:** the search worker now has the one-step `search`/`collect` tools. Its goal asks only for every hit of the catalogue group and no longer shows the requirement. Showing it was the cause of the loops: the model kept refining when the hit didn't fit yet (507 vs 500 mm before the amendment and reserve). If a search worker still stops, a grounded reader takes the hit list from the pages it already read.
- **Material details:** `open_pages` skips views already read, lists the links not yet opened, and recovers once if the session dropped. The goal asks for every view link in the first call and one more call after that. Detail workers now take 4 requests each.
- **Step limits:** they scale with the time left (about 5 s per step). Search is 6 to 16 steps, details 6 to 18 instead of the fixed 12/14.
- **Session handling:**
  - Tabs are reused instead of opened fresh, and new tabs open one at a time.
  - Sign-ins are serialised: a tab that waited while another signed in just reloads.
  - After about 120 s idle, one tab checks the session before the workers start again.
  - A dropped session gets one gate pass shared by all tabs, triggered only on a sign-in form or a known gate page. Some views redirect, and the first version mistook that for a dropped session.
- **Deadline:** material workers stop early enough for their reader call, so finished records are kept. The new `Erp.so_far()`, which main's orchestrator already calls, returns searched requirements whose materials weren't all read marked as unfinished. If no read material matches, the position is omitted, never turned into no_bid.
- **reselect:** a choice the ERP stage already made that still passes `check_choice` stands without a model call. Other choices run thinking off with a 30 s timeout inside a 50 s budget, and choices made before a timeout are kept. A failed or late choice returns None with a problem attached, so the position is omitted rather than read as no_bid. Stale "stroke pending" problems are cleared. `agent/tests/test_reselect.py` covers this.

**Timings (local, read-only, with the stroke reserves passed)**

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| ERP stage | 288 s, 3 detail workers at the 12-step limit | 209 s |
| Search | 7 to 16 steps | 3 to 5 steps |
| Details | often 12 (limit) | 4 steps |
| reselect | ~95 s (one thinking call hung to its 90 s timeout) | 4.5 s |

Materials and records matched the hand derivation in `checks/derivation.md` for A-1, A-3, A-4 and A-5. A-2 shows no material locally only because `research_check.py` passes no amendments. Later local runs were too noisy to time: other agents share the scouting login, which keeps dropping the session, and the model proxy returned 502/503s.

- **First run** (before the search fix): auto-restarted after a platform failure as `run_39383b2090680754`, **quality 51.2**. A-2's search looped for 16 steps and the position was omitted.
- **Second run** `run_ff6a98847ab46e6e`: **quality 100**, eligible, 12 min, $0.167.
  - All five searches were done about 30 s after starting.
  - Every detail worker that finished took 4 to 5 requests, about 20 s each.
  - The final choice was instant for A-1, A-3 and A-5 and took 2 to 4 s for A-2 and A-4.
  - A-2's search still hit 16 steps; it still got HZ-2170, and the only explanation I have is the hit-list salvage (the log is truncated).
  - The non-chosen HZ-9704 hit 18 steps.
  - Both results are recorded in `experiments/runs.jsonl`, and `docs/learned.md` has a bullet.

**The two soak runs you flagged** (`run_0139c00e4530c09d`, `run_19a63c64cf9b9588`, on main's code): what I could pull of the logs is truncated (only the tail). The stops I can see were detail workers that clicked through tabs one by one, plus maintenance popups, and hit 12 requests after 115 to 180 s, not 11 to 17 s. I couldn't see the A-2/A-5 search stops; on main those are the searches that refine after a non-fitting hit. <result>Neither Haiku run reached quality 100: run 1 scored 54 and run 2 scored 42.1. The Anthropic backend itself worked: all 634 Claude calls answered 200, with no 429s and no 400s. Because both runs fell short, I did not start a third, and I made no code changes.

**Setup**
- The team secret `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` exists (checked by name only).
- Local smoke call through the backend, one typed answer each: thinking off 1.9 s, thinking on 4.2 s.
- The export of `8cdb0cb` is at `/Users/jonasstenglein/.claude/jobs/064ce75e/tmp/haiku-export/agent`. Only its `agent.json` run command was changed, and the dry run showed no `.env`.
- Both runs are recorded in the main repo's `experiments/runs.jsonl` with `--experiment model-haiku-5-5 --commit 8cdb0cb --dirty no`. Not committed.

| | Haiku run 1 `run_c22233db5d990663` | Haiku run 2 `run_6916caf75cb4fefd` | Qwen at 100 `run_262c91398cd64598` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Quality | 54 | 42.1 | 100 |
| Duration | 7:29 | 9:00 | 7:33 |
| Cost | $0.129 (345 calls) | $0.090 (289 calls) | $0.110 (304 calls) |
| Latency p50 / p95 | 1.5 s / 4.8 s | 1.5 s / 3.3 s | 3.6 s / 18 s |
| Total time inside model calls | 15:23 | 13:11 | 29:50 (22:27 of it queue wait) |

**Cost:** all of it is Anthropic; the runs made no Qwen calls. Prompt caching works: the platform counts about 2.2k–2.8k uncached input tokens per run and about 1.1M cached. Per-run cost ends up about the same as Qwen.

**Speed:** Haiku answers about twice as fast per call with no queue. The runs were not shorter overall, because website steps and the mail search budgets set the wall time.

**Thinking modes:** both work. The kept logs show 103 thinking-on calls (median 2.0 s, max 7 s) and the rest thinking-off, all 200.

**The 401s are harmless (inferred).** Each run ends with two `401 authentication_error` lines from the sign/submit worker, 0.1 s after the TAN was typed and submit clicked. The bid was already signed and scored at that point, and the platform shows "Failures: none" for both runs. So the proxy most likely refuses calls once the run token closes at submission, which is not a key or backend problem.

**Score breakdown**
- **Run 1:** decision, tender reference and discount scored; positions 01.02–01.04 got full points. 01.01 and 01.05 got 0, and so did the total, withdrawn answer and metal index window.
  - 01.01 was left out of the form; the form worker reported it as omitted.
  - 01.05: the form worker left the certification field empty three times. On 01.04 it correctly chose "Ohne Zertifizierung". That looks like a Haiku form-filling mistake (thinking off).
  - The verifier wanted no_bid; it was overruled.
- **Run 2:** 01.01 got full points and 01.05 got 9.1, missing surcharge, rebate and unit price. 01.02–01.04 got 0, along with the total and the index window; the withdrawn answer scored.
  - The answer contained only 01.01 and 01.05.

**Known main bugs versus Haiku**
- **Known bugs:** both runs logged "A-2 / A-4 stroke pending (per-end reserve …)". In run 2 that matches the missing positions. Run 2 also lost 210 s waiting on the webmail sign-in ("still loading"). Mail searches hit the 20-step cap, and the setup-pool search ran out of budget once in each run.
- **Probably Haiku:** the metal index window scored 0 in both runs, although the right index mail was found and read; the Qwen run at 100 scored it. Plus the empty certification on 01.05 in run 1.
- Separating these cleanly needs a Haiku rerun once the reserve and ERP fixes are on main.

**Next steps (your call)**
- Rebase this branch on main once the reliability fixes land, then run Haiku again.
- Optionally try thinking on for the form step (`THINKING_FORM=1`) and the readers.
- Only after that would a third run say much.</result>

<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Overlap bid form with research" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>I overlapped the bid form with the research. One sandbox test run of the branch (run_07b516589180c4bb) scored 100 and signed at about 620 s, so roughly 10:20 total. The usual full-score time on main is about 11.6 min. That is one run, so treat the saving as indicative. Main is merged in (latest 980ba45) and all unit tests pass.

**Design**
- **Early bid worker:** right after the briefing, `BidFlow` starts in `agent/submit.py`. The model enters the bidder data, with the contact address from the webmail lookup that now starts at the same time. It then picks the main offer and stops on the first position page without entering anything there. On that page it calls a new tool, `map_position_form()`, which records which control takes which value and which buttons apply a choice and save. Everything is checked against the page. In the sandbox this pass took about 50 s and finished long before the research did.
- **Waiting:** the tab fetches the site's start page every 40 s from inside the page, which keeps the session alive without navigating. If the session is lost anyway, the page is reloaded and the early pass runs again, up to 3 times.
  - Checked locally: after a 180 s idle tab and after repeated fetches, a reload still showed the position page.
  - The first version used Playwright's `context.request`, which cannot resolve the site addresses in the sandbox (every keepalive failed); that is fixed and checked locally both ways.
- **Hand-off:** `flow.finish(answer, attributes)` replaces the old `submit_bid` call in `solve()`. If the early pass is still running it waits up to 45 s, then cancels it. The existing form worker then continues from the position page and calls `configure_position()` with just the position number.
  - If the decision is no_bid, the worker is told to go back to the offer-type step and pick the declaration; only empty steps get cleared.
  - If the wizard was reset, it redoes the steps from the start.
  - The deadline guard and signing reserve are unchanged.
  - `AGENT_FORM_OVERLAP=0` turns the overlap off.

**Other time savings**
- **Dead decision check:** `confirm_decision` at the end of the orchestrator could never change the outcome, because calc never returns a bid that has missing materials. It cost about 8 s before every form, so it is now skipped. This is the only orchestrator change.
- **Option picks reused:** when the model maps a value to a differently worded option once (the "Keine" certification shown as "Ohne Zertifizierung"), that pick now carries to later positions with the same control and value. Previously each such position failed once, about 10 s each.
- **Form status not grounded:** a form status that quoted our own discount "5,00" was rejected four times (about 30 s) in a local run, and the model then went back a step looking for it. Those status results are no longer checked against page text.

**Summary-check fix for run_0d2decb7f0401931:** once the review page is reached, signing now gets only the TAN tool, no requirements to "fix", and 15 steps. It must never go back, but may go forward to the signing step. In that run a false alarm sent signing back and forth for about 100 s. The summary check now runs alongside signing and only goes into the logs, which also removes about 3 s of waiting.

**Measured (sandbox run, quality 100)**

| Phase | Before (earlier 100 runs) | This run |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Form, from research end to review page | 132–185 s | 91 s |
| Signing | 25–30 s | 30 s |

Research ended at about 499 s and the bid was signed about 121 s later. On the local scouting sites, the time from the first position page to the last position went from about 31 s to about 11 s.

Local end-to-end measurement was unreliable. The tender portal session dropped every 1–3 minutes while other runs logged in with the shared scouting login, and each drop reset the wizard to step 1. The model proxy also returned errors and hung several calls for 45 s. The new code handled both, but they make local timing noisy.

Branch `worktree-agent-ac9d354450edd835d`, 8 commits on main (not pushed or merged):
- `Skip the decision check when it cannot change it`
- `Start the bid form while the research runs`
- `Reuse an option pick for the same value`
- `Retry the early form pass after an error`
- `Sign a reached review as it stands`
- `Do not ground the form worker's status result`
- `Keep the portal session alive from the tab`
- `Record the form-overlap test run` (adds the run to `experiments/runs.jsonl` and notes to `docs/learned.md`)

The code changes are in `agent/submit.py`, `agent/configurator.py`, `agent/agent.py`, the end of `Orchestrator.run()` in `agent/orchestrator.py`, and the new `agent/tests/test_bid_flow.py`.

The sandbox run was uploaded at the "Sign a reached review" commit, so the last two code fixes (grounding and in-tab keepalive) have unit and local tests but no sandbox run yet. One more test run would confirm them and the time saving.</result>

<task-id>a5dca58447b78345c</task-id>
<tool-use-id>toolu_01HxSN2eLrpn39v4ibdppZ8c</tool-use-id>
<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jonasstenglein-OtherProjects-EHL-Finale-error404ai-ehl-finals/064ce75e-f288-40e2-8ed8-853ec224ce81/tasks/a5dca58447b78345c.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Private-split generalization audit" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found 20 risks for the private run. The top one is a real bug, not a tuning risk: if the ERP reader misses a lead time for any chosen material, the whole bid is submitted as no_bid. Everything below was checked in the source at 1083c2e. The four items marked "(ran it)" were also reproduced with small calc/evidence snippets. Nothing was edited, committed or run against the sites.

## Ranked risks

**1. A missing lead time turns the whole bid into no_bid (bug, ran it).**
- **Where:** `orchestrator.py:832-841`. `to_material()` raises `CalcError` when the lead time from stock or the production lead time is unknown (`evidence.py:301-304`). The except branch then builds `calc.PositionInput` without a material, and `calc.calculate` (`calc.py:544-548`) returns no_bid for the whole bid.
- **Triggers:** the reader misses a lead-time view, or `parse_weeks` fails (for example "10 Arbeitstage" is not whole weeks). `check_choice` never checks lead times.
- **Impact:** about −90 to −95 in a bid scenario.
- **Fix:** in that branch, leave the position out (or fill the lead time with its own field blanked); never append a material-less position.

**2. Real no_bid cases are omitted instead of declared.**
- **Where:**
  - An empty catalogue-group search gives `inspected=[]`. `choose_material` then returns "no material could be inspected" (`research.py:1242-1243`) and `build_bid` omits the position as "ERP not researched" (`orchestrator.py:812-813`).
  - Any research problem also leads to omission rather than no_bid (`orchestrator.py:819-822`), for example "1 of 6 materials not read" or "N hits, inspected the first 10".
- **Impact:** in a no_bid scenario the decision alone is worth 75, so we keep 5 to 25 of 100.
- **Fix:**
  - Add a `group_empty` flag to `SearchReport`, set only when the site's own no-hits text is shown (`site_tools.NO_HITS`), and treat it as a completed search with no match.
  - Before signing a bid that has positions omitted for "material undecided", run one thinking `confirm_decision`.

**3. A false no_bid is never double-checked (ran it).**
- **Where:** `norm_text` only turns a few words into "none" (`evidence.py:154`). "keine Zertifizierung", "ohne Zertifikat" and "nicht erforderlich" stay as text, so they mismatch an ERP "-". The model must then answer '' and `calc` declares no_bid. `confirm_decision` only runs for a bid (`orchestrator.py:887`).
- **Impact:** about −90 in a bid scenario.
- **Fix:**
  - Normalise any phrase that starts with kein/keine/ohne/nicht/no/none to "none".
  - Before accepting no_bid, ask a thinking model whether the mismatches are only wording, and confirm the decision.

**4. The bid form is assumed to show one position per page with native controls.**
- **Where:**
  - One label mapping is reused for every position (`submit.py` PositionDesk).
  - `find_control` returns the first exact label match (`configurator.py:111-113`), so on a table with repeated "Menge" labels every position is written into row 1.
  - Only native `select` and `textbox` controls are recognised (`configurator.py:17`, `fill_position`). Comboboxes and listboxes cannot be mapped.
  - The goal forbids the model from entering values itself (`submit.py:423-428`).
  - The cascade only knows bore, stroke, pressure, mounting and certification (`orchestrator.py:214-221`, `configurator.py:25-28`). A "Kataloggruppe" or series select blocks the material choice.
- **Impact:** up to −65, since every position can be lost.
- **Fix:**
  - Scope labels to the row or container that shows the position number.
  - Add catalogue group to the attributes passed to the form.
  - After two structural failures, let the model fill that position itself with `fill_in()` using the given values.

**5. German number format is hardcoded in the form.**
- **Where:** `configurator.py:52-64` and `312-314`; the prompts at `submit.py:348`, `393`, `402` and `415`. `extract.py` shows `input[type=number]` as a plain "textbox", and Playwright's `fill("1234,50")` on such an input fails. The brief states the format ("in German number format"), but we ignore whatever it says.
- **Impact:** every position fails and the total is wrong, up to −65.
- **Fix:** take the format from `briefing.submission_steps` and the input type; on type=number use a dot; check the field by parsing the number, not by string equality.

**6. Scoped amendments are matched by exact string (ran it).**
- **Where:** `calc.py:354` compares revision and requirement with `!=`. "Rev. C" vs "C" silently drops the amendment. The result is a wrong material or a false no_bid (see item 3).
- **Fix:** compare with `research.same_revision` and an id normaliser. The reserves already use a looser match (`orchestrator._same_id`).

**7. Q&amp;A on a different website than the bill of quantities is lost.**
- **Where:** `research_tender.py:494-496` and `534-536` return None when no positions are found, which throws away that site's Q&amp;A. Two tender workers also overwrite each other's `tender_facts` (`orchestrator.py:331-333`).
- **Scenario:** the private dataset splits the information differently.
- **Impact:** about −20 to −40: quantities, the withdrawn check, keys, amendments, packaging/pool/bonus/delivery.
- **Fix:** return Q&amp;A-only facts and merge per site (positions from one site, Q&amp;A from another).

**8. The TAN lookup prompt describes public-1's mailbox.**
- **Where:** `submit.py:97-104`: "listed without a search, newest first", "highest request number", `max_steps=10`, thinking off.
- **Impact:** an unsigned bid scores about 0.
- **Fix:** drop the layout claims; allow searching for the reference or "TAN"; allow about 16 steps with thinking on for a retry.

**9. Only the first 10 search hits are inspected.**
- **Where:** `research.py:763` and `1084`, while rule 10 allows up to 20 hits.
- **Impact:** a match at hit 11 or later omits that position (one position's share, about −13 at 5 positions).
- **Fix:** pre-filter the hits by the bore and pressure their rows show; raise the cap to 20.

**10. Stroke reserves are keyed by reserve key only, not by revision.**
- **Where:** `research_mail.py:684-686`, then the revision filter at `orchestrator.py:373-378`.
- **Scenario:** a later Technikfreigabe for another revision overwrites ours by key, the stroke stays pending, and the position is omitted.
- **Fix:** key the reserves by (key, normalised revision).

**11. Only one setup pool and one bonus plan are supported.**
- **Where:** `research_mail.py:899-917`; `calc.BidInput` has a single `setup_pool` and `project_bonus`.
- **Impact:** members of a second pool or plan lose surcharge or rebate (10% each per position).
- **Fix:** make these lists in calc and allocate per key.

**12. A pieces_per_set amendment is parsed but never applied.**
- **Where:** `effective_requirement` ignores it (`evidence.py:227-242`), and `build_bid` passes the unamended requirement (`orchestrator.py:833`).
- **Impact:** a set position's piece count is wrong, along with its scale, packaging, pool and bonus.
- **Fix:** apply amended `pieces_per_set` in `to_position_input`.

**13. Identifiers are compared exactly.**
- **Where:**
  - Q&amp;A position and answer numbers: `research_tender.py:549-556` and `574`, `calc.py:318-321`. "Pos. 2" vs "2", or "Nr. 07" vs "7", means a quantity change or withdrawal is missed and the 5-point withdrawn check is lost.
  - Delivery commitment material number: `calc.py:535`.
- **Fix:** one normaliser (strip the prefix, the leading zeros and any zero-width characters) used for all of these.

**14. Several parsers break on formats public-1 does not use.**
- `parse_unit("Stücke")` raises (ran it, `calc.py:92-99`). It runs inside `resolve_tender` (`research_tender.py:592`) without a per-position guard, so all tender facts are lost.
- Currency is cut to three characters (`research.py:713`), so "€" becomes "€" rather than EUR, and FX lookup fails.
- `parse_date` reads neither month names nor two-digit years (`evidence.py:111-131`).
- `parse_weeks` divides working days by 7 (`evidence.py:147`).
- **Fix:** per-position try with a "piece" default and a problem logged; map €, Euro and $ to ISO codes; extend `parse_date`.

**15. FX direction and metal-index unit are copied as written.**
- **Where:** `research_mail.py:132` and `90`. The grounding check forbids inverted values, so "1 EUR = 1,08 USD" or a rate per tonne cannot be converted.
- **Fix:** add direction and unit fields and convert in `_apply`.

**16. The planner may leave out a fact kind.**
- **Where:** the validator only checks site ids (`orchestrator.py:109-114`); the prompt invites leaving out what the rules do not require.
- **Fix:** require all four kinds in the validator.

**17. Fixed time budgets.**
- **Where:** `SUBMIT_SECONDS` 390 and `FORM_SECONDS` 300 (`orchestrator.py:50-51`); `SIGNING_RESERVE` 180; the "time is short" cut at `submit.py:281`.
- **Scenario:** public-1 took about 91 s of form for 5 positions. At 12 or more positions the later ones get cut. ERP time also grows with more groups.
- **Fix:** scale the research cutoff with the number of positions.

**18. No PDF or ZIP reading.**
- Nothing in `extract.py` or `keep_pages` reads either format. The brief mentions ZIP files.
- **Scenario:** a bill of quantities that exists only as PDF or ZIP means no positions at all.

**19. The no_bid form path depends on going back.**
- **Where:** the early BidFlow pass already picks the main offer (`submit.py` BidFlow goal). A no_bid then has to go back (`submit.py:388-393`). This has only been tested in a mock, and the summary check is logged, not enforced.
- **Fix:** stop the early pass before the offer-type step, or enforce the check when the decision is no_bid.

**20. An approval sender given by name matches the display name only** (`research_mail.py:661`). This is low risk: the public brief gives a local part.

## Already handled correctly in calc

FX before rounding, HALF_UP at the named steps, the inclusive threshold with no fallback, conditional approval giving 0 for no_bid, metal-index windows, largest remainder, pooled stock, the commitment ceiling, and setup/packaging never discounted. `test_calc.py` covers these (44 tests).

## What dev/robustness.py covers

It covers renamed or shuffled site ids, product names, a reworded or reordered brief and masked URLs. It checks the briefing, the mailbox address, one mail lookup and a tender positions read.

It does not cover spec, ERP, mail kinds or calc traps, other layouts or labels, number formats, the configurator, the no_bid path, split sites, or larger scenarios.</result>

<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jonasstenglein-OtherProjects-EHL-Finale-error404ai-ehl-finals/064ce75e-f288-40e2-8ed8-853ec224ce81/tasks/ad91eb003002241ee.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Fix slow site starts" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Site starts that hang are now detected and recovered within about 15 to 20 seconds instead of minutes. Both sandbox test runs of the fix scored 100, but neither hit a hung start, so the recovery path is so far proven only by the local tests.

**Root cause.** In the failing run, the webmail's requests never completed in its own browser context, while a fresh context opened the site in under a second. So the site was fine and that one context was stuck.
- **Attempt 1 (about:blank):** the page load never got its response. I reproduced locally that Playwright calls like `locator.count()` and `evaluate()` then wait forever with no timeout. `_settle` makes those calls (first in the sign-in helper, which checks for a password field), so it hit its 45 s cap over and over. On top of that, `_attempt` went straight back to settling after each timeout instead of checking whether the page was already usable.
- **Attempt 2:** the sign-in was sent but never came back. The platform's recording shows no second sign-in being typed, which fits a sign-in step stuck on the same kind of call.
- The Haiku run `run_6916caf75cb4fefd` shows the same thing on the webmail: the login page loaded, then nothing for 210 s.

**Changes** (in `agent/browser_tools.py`, nothing branches on site id, URL or layout):
- **Race:** if a start has no usable page after 15 s, a second tab opens next to it in a fresh context. The first tab stays open, so a site that is merely slow is not given up. Whichever shows a usable page first wins and the other tab and context are closed.
- **The first tab is the session itself:** only the racing tab runs as a separate session that is taken over if it wins. This keeps the ERP research's own per-tab sign-in (one tab signs in at a time) and `revive()` working as before.
- **Dead page:** a loaded page that answers no script call for 20 s ends its tab. A navigation that hasn't loaded yet keeps waiting, because the race covers that case.
- **Budgets:** attempts get 45/60/90/120 s (previously 210 s each). Retries start after 0/10/30 s (previously 30/60/120 s), each in a fresh context.
- **Usable page counts as a start:** if settling times out, the page is still read, and a page showing content counts as a successful start.
- **Settle helpers:** each helper is capped at 15 s and skipped when the page doesn't answer. `SETTLE_SECONDS` stays at 45.
- **During research:** `GOTO_SECONDS` went from 60 to 30 s. Every page open now runs through `_goto`, which logs slow ones as `slow_page` events.

**Tests:** all 197 unit tests pass. The new `agent/tests/test_fast_start.py` uses a local server and covers:
- a page load that never gets a response;
- a sign-in that never comes back;
- a page that stops answering script calls;
- a page that shows content but never finishes loading;
- a site that is slow for every tab, where the first tab must still win;
- browser dialogs on a winning racing tab reaching the session.

I updated `test_slow_start.py` for the new constants.

**Sandbox runs of 86b4f27:**

| Run | Quality | Time | Cost | `start_ok` seconds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run_6f121a1dc3174d50` | 100 | 7:33 | $0.136 | tender 2.5, customer portal 2.7, mail 2.9, ERP 4.6 |
| `run_412b8440736e3701` | 100 | 11:33 | $0.151 | tender 2.4, customer portal 2.5, mail 3.1, ERP 4.4 |

Worker tabs started in 0.8 to 2.5 s in both runs.

In the second run, three webmail tabs mid-research hit page loads that never got a response. That is the same symptom during research, not at the start. Each was now cut off after about 52 s (30 s load plus about 22 s settle) and logged as `slow_page`; before, there was no such log and the load alone could take up to 60 s.

- `a3c61fc` Record fast-starts test runs: 100 and 100 (both runs in `experiments/runs.jsonl`, plus a note on the root cause in `docs/learned.md`)</result>

<task-id>acdfec2d75a7ea016</task-id>
<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jonasstenglein-OtherProjects-EHL-Finale-error404ai-ehl-finals/064ce75e-f288-40e2-8ed8-853ec224ce81/tasks/acdfec2d75a7ea016.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Gap-filling second research pass" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>I built the gap-filling second pass, and both coordinator issues (the pending stroke reserves and the mail kinds that stop at their budget) are covered. The branch is `worktree-agent-acdfec2d75a7ea016`, rebased onto main 987d8ee, and the unit tests pass. I have not pushed or merged it. Of six sandbox test runs on earlier versions, 2 scored 100. The final head f4443a5 has not had a sandbox run yet.

**Design.** Almost all of it is in a new `agent/gaps.py`, so it stays out of the other agents' modules.
- **Where it runs:** in `orchestrator.run()`, after the first calc, `GapFiller` lists what the answer still lacks:
  - a requirement that was not read;
  - an ERP search with no result or no decision;
  - a stroke reserve still pending;
  - a chosen material missing a record or facts it needs;
  - a key from the Q&amp;A that no mail record resolved (condition group, packaging, commitment, setup pool, bonus);
  - no approval, no FX rate or no metal index;
  - no tender positions at all.
- **Retries:** it runs targeted retries side by side, using the same model-driven workers as the first pass:
  - a spec navigator told exactly which requirement ids are missing;
  - the ERP requirement or material worker again;
  - the missing mail kinds on fresh tabs;
  - the tender again.
- **Rounds:** after each round it re-chooses the affected materials and recalculates. It does up to 3 rounds and at most 2 tries per gap.
- **Time guard:** a round starts only with at least 75 s of research time left, and every retry is cut 30 s before the research deadline. The form and the signing reserve are never touched.
- **Never loses a fact:** results add to what the first pass found, and the more complete answer is kept. If the pass itself fails, the first answer stands.
- **Trace:** each gap shows up as `gap`, `gap_retry`, `gap_filled`, `gap_round` and `gap_open` events, and these appear in the run summary.

**Fixes driven by the runs:**
- **Tender watch** (`orchestrator.py`): if no positions arrive within 240 s, a second tender attempt runs on fresh tabs and the first to finish with positions wins. In run ecfd1989 the positions reader kept timing out from 137 s until the research deadline, so the bid had no positions. In run f804d0ff the watch fired at 259 s, the retry won at 380 s, and the run scored 100.
- **Stroke reserves still pending at calc time** (soak runs 0139c00e and 19a63c64): the cause was that the mail plan call timed out, so no mail kind was searched at all. Main's own plan retry now covers that; I took main's version and only kept the tab cleanup. In addition, a requirement still waiting on its reserve gets an estimated material instead of being omitted: the active inspected material with the smallest stroke above the usable travel. It can be wrong, but it keeps the position's quantity and other points. The gap pass still searches for the reserve.
- **Packaging read cut by the mail deadline** (run ca6366f9): the message was found at 508 s, but the mail deadline cut its reading. The gap pass now reads such kept messages again (a model call, no browsing) before searching that kind once more.
- **Verifier doubts:** they are almost always "the quote shows only the header". The rework of doubted mail kinds now happens inside the gap round instead of in its own round before calc. Doubted kinds only use mail tabs that real gaps leave free, and doubts alone never start a round. This came from two runs: in f04e4b35, eight doubted kinds held all four tabs and the one real gap (a setup pool) stayed open; in 03b3344d, a doubt-only round delayed a 100 answer by 190 s.

**Scores.** All six runs are recorded in `experiments/runs.jsonl`.

| Run | Score | What happened |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1c1dbf3b | 60.3 | Research complete, no gaps; the form lost 01.04 and the surcharges |
| ecfd1989 | 10 | Tender reader hung; led to the tender watch |
| f04e4b35 | 82.2 | Setup pool gap left open; doubted kinds held the mail tabs |
| f804d0ff | 100 | Tender retry rescued the positions |
| 03b3344d | 100 | Full answer, but the doubt-only round cost 190 s |
| 4e5b0873 | 0, not eligible | Tender portal and webmail very slow; neither tender attempt read positions and signing failed in the webmail |

One local run with mail time cut to 200 s scored 59.2. The pass had only 86 s and filled FX and metal index; the ERP retry for A-5 was cut at the deadline.

**Limits and hooks for other modules:**
- **Late start:** the pass only begins after verify and select. With slow sites the first pass often finishes around 550 to 700 s, which leaves 0 to 140 s for retries.
- **Bounded reader:** the tender positions reader in `research_tender.py` can retry 180 s calls for about 10 minutes. It needs a total bound.
- **Retry hint:** `Erp.requirement` could take an optional `hint=` argument; `gaps.py` already passes one if that parameter exists.
- **Run 0e7ccbf8:** the platform log kept no steps of the approval searches, so I could not find why they read nothing. In that run mail ran until 680 s, so there was no time left for a gap round anyway.
- **Commit message:** commit 1ddfac0 is titled "Retry the mail plan" but after the rebase it only cancels the mail tabs when the plan fails and adds the stroke estimate. I left the message as it is.</result>

<tool-use-id>toolu_01JhkiffrP2WNWX3pEhsLiLw</tool-use-id>
<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jonasstenglein-OtherProjects-EHL-Finale-error404ai-ehl-finals/064ce75e-f288-40e2-8ed8-853ec224ce81/tasks/a3a2adb02e0132161.output</output-file>

<summary>Agent "Fix decision and parsing risks" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>All the audit fixes are in, all 229 unit tests pass, and both sandbox test runs scored quality 100 with every field correct.

The work is committed on branch `worktree-agent-a3a2adb02e0132161`: the code as `fdb0272`, the two run records as `bdba595`. Nothing is merged or pushed. My worktree started at 39c95a8, so I first fast-forwarded it to main 1083c2e, the commit the audit was based on.

**Changes per item**

1. **Reading gaps no longer turn the whole bid into no_bid.**
   - A missing lead time now fails only the position that actually needs it, and that position is left out. Which lead time is needed depends on the combined demand of all positions using the material, so calc does this check (`calc.py`, `evidence.py`, `orchestrator.py`).
   - The fallback that added a position without a material is gone. A material that was found but whose record could not be read, or whose unit is unreadable, now leaves its position out.
   - A position without a material is only added when "no matching material" was actually established.
   - Lead times: "Arbeitstage"/"Werktage"/"business days" convert at 5 a week and calendar days at 7. Months or counts that are not whole weeks are treated as unknown instead of wrong. The rules page only says "lead time" in weeks.
2. **An empty catalogue group now supports no_bid.**
   - The search tool in `site_tools.py` now records each search's query, row count and whether the site showed its own no-hits notice.
   - A group counts as empty only when the worker reports no hits, no search listed any rows, at least two searches showed the notice, and one of them searched for the group itself. This is deliberately strict, so a search typed into the wrong field cannot cause a false no_bid.
   - Such a group is passed to the bid as "searched, no match". Everything else is still treated as a reading gap and the position is left out.
3. **False no_bid is double-checked.**
   - Negation phrases now count as "no requirement": "keine Zertifizierung", "ohne Zertifikat", "nicht erforderlich", "not required", "entfällt" and similar. A value containing a digit never does, and "nicht rostend" stays a real value.
   - Before a no_bid caused by missing materials is accepted, the agent looks for active materials that match on every number and differ only in mounting or certification wording. If there are any, one thinking call (`verify.py`) judges whether the difference is only wording.
   - The no_bid is overturned only when that check gives exactly one material for every missing position. Otherwise it stands, and when no such candidate exists no model call is made.
   - The existing `confirm_decision` for bids is unchanged.
6. **Amendments match normalised labels.** "Rev. C", "Revision C" and "C" now count as the same revision, and the same applies to requirement ids. "B-1" vs "B" and "AB" vs "B" still do not match.
12. **Amended pieces per set are applied.** An amended set size now overrides the spec's value when quantities in sets are converted to pieces.
13. **Position, answer and material numbers are normalised everywhere they are compared.** "Pos. 2" equals "2" and "07" equals "7"; prefixes, leading zeros, zero-width characters and whitespace are ignored. This covers quantity changes, withdrawals, the Q&amp;A keys, setup fees, amendments, setup pool and bonus members, and the material number of a delivery commitment. Material numbers keep their letters, so a look-alike material number still does not match.
14. **Parsers:**
   - Units accept "Stücke", "Stk.", "Teile" and "Kit". An unreadable unit, quantity or fee now costs only its own position, and the problem is logged in the tender facts and the trace.
   - Currencies map to ISO codes (€/Euro, $/US-Dollar, £, Franken and so on). Unknown words are kept whole instead of cut to three letters.
   - Dates accept German and English month names and two-digit years.

**Tests**

New file `agent/tests/test_decision_robustness.py` with 27 tests, using invented values only. The full suite (229 tests) passes.

|---|---|---|---|

- `research.py`: the search loop (empty-group detection), the currency line, and an empty-group shortcut in `reselect()`.
- `orchestrator.py`: `safe_calculate`, `build_bid`, `run()`, and two new methods, `_calculate` and `wording_matches`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Fix A-2 ERP search loop" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>The search on a group with a single hit now finishes in 4–5 model steps (16–25 s), down from running to the 16-step cap. No detail worker reached a cap in either test run. Both sandbox runs scored quality 100. The branch is at `86b3868`, merged with main `2686f6c`; all 247 unit tests pass. Nothing is pushed.

**Cause.** The run logs only show the browser actions, so I reproduced the workers locally against the ERP with a probe that printed every model call and tool result. Two things went wrong:
- **The model did not trust a one-row result.** It called `collect` on it, searched the same group again, then shortened the query (`GR-515` in `run_9f6e…`) and paged through that wider list. The search tool output never said the list was finished.
- **The ERP's "Wartungshinweis" maintenance pop-up sometimes wiped the query.** When it re-rendered the form, the query went out empty (`q=` in the URL) and the tool returned the page footer as "rows". The model then saw "1 row", then "2 rows", then "1 row" again for the same query, and kept retrying.

**Detail workers.** Locally every detail worker finished in 4–5 steps. I could not see the steps of the sandbox cases that hit 18, so that fix is a guard rather than a fix of an observed cause.

**Changes** (all generic; nothing from the sites in code):
- **Search tool (`agent/site_tools.py`):**
  - It resends a query once when the result page doesn't show it.
  - It drops rows without links when the site says there are no hits.
  - It ends a finished list with "This is the complete result list: N hits, no further page."
  - It records each search (query, rows, no-hits notice, complete, text, time) on the session.
- **Search worker (`agent/research.py`):**
  - The goal tells the model to return as soon as it sees that line, and to stop after the group itself shows no hits in the available fields and spellings.
  - New harness stop: `until_settled` ends the worker 20 s after it has a complete hit list for the group (`ERP_SETTLE_SECONDS`). The salvage reader then reads that list.
  - The same stop applies after a second no-hits search for the group.
- **Detail workers:**
  - `open_pages` now tracks unopened links across all its calls and says "No unopened links remain" when none are left.
  - The harness ends the worker 20 s after that, and the reader reads what was opened.
- **Your inspection cap item:**
  - Up to 20 hits are now inspected (`MAX_INSPECT` 20).
  - Hits whose row attributes fit the requirement as known so far go first. Attributes a row doesn't show, and a stroke still pending, never count against a hit.
  - The other hits are still inspected, because a later amendment can change the requirement. They come last, and once one of the fitting hits is chosen they are not waited for, so the common case is no slower.
- **Your empty-group item:** after the merge I dropped my `group_empty` field and reused main's `RequirementResearch.no_hits` and `evidence.group_without_hits()`.
  - The search records stay dicts with `query`, `rows` and `no_hits`, plus my `complete`, `text` and `at`.
  - A search whose query never reached the site and that has no links counts as 0 rows, so a swallowed search can't block empty-group detection.
  - `no_hits` is now also set when the search worker stops without returning anything.

**Tests.** New file `agent/tests/test_erp_converge.py` with 12 tests:
- the complete-list line for one hit;
- resending a swallowed query;
- dropping footer rows and recording the no-hits notice;
- `until_settled` ending a worker and keeping a quick result;
- inspection order and the 20 cap;
- the early choice not waiting for the other hits, and `no_hits` set or cleared;
- the `open_pages` done signal on a local server.

**What the docs say** (unchanged since my last read):
- faq.md lists it: "bring your own key for any provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, Google, Groq, Mistral, DeepSeek, xAI, Together, **Fireworks**, …) and send its calls through the metering proxy."
- run-contract.md gives the route: "`fireworks` | `https://api.fireworks.ai/inference` | `$LLM_PROXY_URL/fireworks/v1`".
- The run gets no `FIREWORKS_BASE_URL`. Its list of base-URL variables names only Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, Mistral and Together, so the client would have to build `$LLM_PROXY_URL/fireworks/v1` itself.
- The key goes as `Authorization: Bearer` ("OpenAI and compatible providers"). `FIREWORKS_API_KEY` is an allowed secret name.
- The catch, from run-contract.md: "Elsewhere, unpriced models and models outside a configured allowlist are refused before forwarding." faq.md adds that models must "satisfy any organizer model allowlist".
- fair-play.md: "calling a model other than through our proxy" is not allowed. So we may not call Fireworks any other way.

**Probe results** (fake key; the local proxy on 127.0.0.1:8765 only serves Qwen and Clef, so I used the team dev endpoint):
- `accounts/fireworks/models/glm-5p3-flash` is refused by the proxy itself with `unknown model`, before anything is forwarded. A made-up model id gets exactly the same answer.
- Every other Fireworks id I tried is refused the same way: `glm-5p3-flash`, `glm-5p3`, `glm-5p1`, `glm-4p6`, `deepseek-v3p1`, `llama-v3p1-8b-instruct`, `kimi-k2-instruct`, `gpt-oss-120b`. The Fireworks route currently seems to have no priced or allowed models.
- Other routes do forward. Earlier, Anthropic answered with its own "invalid x-api-key", and Groq now answers "Invalid API Key".
- The OpenRouter route passes the request on as well, `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` included. It answered "Missing Authentication header" for every id, including a made-up one, so whether that model id exists there is unconfirmed.

**Branch:** I merged main (01435a7) into my branch, which was a fast-forward and needed no commit. So the branch now equals main.

**Options**
- Ask the organizers whether `accounts/fireworks/models/glm-5p3-flash` can be added to the price list or allowlist. If it is, the backend takes about an hour on the OpenAI client path, using the key below.
- Or run GLM through OpenRouter instead. The proxy forwards OpenRouter calls and OpenRouter reports each call's cost, so pricing would not block it. The secret would then be `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. Say if you want that variant.

<summary>Agent "Generalize bid form for private" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>All four items and the signing guarantee are done. Main (2686f6c) is merged into the branch, the tests pass (257), and the last two sandbox runs both scored quality 100. The branch is `worktree-agent-abf0de9d534fdb5a5`, head 3b89fd2. It is committed only: not merged into main and not pushed.

**Item 4: form shape**
- **Tables and cards:** each control is now taken from the row or card that shows the position number. With repeated labels like "Menge", or row-specific names like `qty_1`, position 1.03 now goes into row 3.
  - Positions in table rows now count as being on the page.
  - When a page lists several positions, the page-level "next" button is clicked only after the last of them is entered.
- **ARIA controls:** comboboxes, listboxes and radio groups now show up in the page view with their label and options, and the filler can pick from them. `fill_in()` can also choose in them. This needed a small change in `extract.py`.
- **Cascade:** the catalogue group now reaches the form. It comes from the requirement, after amendments, through a small hook in `orchestrator.material_attributes`. For a choice nothing is mapped to (a series, say), the model passes `choices={label: option}`. When a list is blocked, the error names those choices with their options.
- **Model fallback:** after two failures caused by the form (not by a missing value), the model gets the position's formatted values and enters it itself with `fill_in()`. It then confirms with `configure_position(..., entered_yourself=True)`. A value the form doesn't offer is still never handed over; that position is omitted.

**Item 5: number format.** The decimal mark is read from the brief's submission steps, defaulting to a comma. Number-only fields always get a dot, in both the filler and `fill_in()`. Fields are checked by comparing numbers rather than strings, and the prompts follow the format the brief asks for.

**Item 8: TAN lookup.** The prompt now describes the goal: the TAN for this tender and request number, newest by request number or mailbox order, never by clock. It may use search, allows 16 steps, and retries once with thinking on.

**Item 19: no-bid.** When the early pass had already picked the main offer, a short pass now goes back to the offer-type step, and the form pass then chooses the declaration. If going back fails, the old hint to go back is kept as a fallback. The same no-bid probe without the early pass also reached the review with the declaration (28 s). I checked the go-back path live on public-1 with a no-bid answer, stopping before the TAN:
- Back at the offer-type step in 5 s.
- The review showed "Verzichtserklärung, Rabatt 0 %, Nettosumme 0,00" 17 s later.

`dev/configurator_check.py` has a new `--prepared` flag to repeat this probe.

**Your priority: always sign**
- **Reserve:** the time kept for signing now grows with the measured model speed (10 steps at the recent pace, at least 180 s, at most 60 % of the time left). The pace is recorded by a 5-line hook in `worker.py`.
- **Sign passes repeat:** a short sign-only pass now runs again until the site confirms or fewer than 45 s are left, so a step cap can no longer end signing with time remaining. That pass may finish an incomplete form: it omits open positions and enters the offer type, bidder data, discount, total and the acknowledgement.
- **401 after submitting:** the platform revokes the model token as soon as the bid is accepted. The loop now treats that 401 as done; one sandbox run had looped on it for its last 10 s.

**Tests.** I added `tests/test_form_shapes.py`, which uses invented pages in a real headless browser, and `tests/test_signing.py`, which replaces the model with scripted workers. They cover each item above, including signing still happening after the form times out and two sign passes hit their step cap.

- **bb71:** the slow mail tab is in `browser_tools`, which another agent owns.
- **0b23:** the merged branch, which includes main's site-start fixes.

The final commit, 95cf29b, only rewords the check that runs before signing, which feeds the run log only. That check had flagged the review's thousands separators ("356.949,62"). This commit was not part of any sandbox run.

<summary>Agent "Generalize mail fact shapes" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>All four gaps are fixed on branch `worktree-agent-a183c69be42b5cc53`. The three sandbox runs all scored 100 and ran faster than earlier v2 runs. One caveat: those runs tested `9cdc47a`, my commit from before I merged main. Main (`3a67031`) then merged cleanly with no conflicts and all 307 unit tests pass, but the merged code has not had its own sandbox run. Nothing is merged into main or pushed.

**Item 11: several setup pools and bonus plans**
- In `calc.BidInput`, setup pools and bonus plans are now lists with one entry per key (`setup_pools`, `project_bonuses`). The old single `setup_pool` and `project_bonus` fields still work and are added to the lists.
- Each pool is split with its own cent rule (rule 25). Each bonus plan applies its bands to its own members' total and splits it by largest remainders (rule 26). A position in more than one pool or plan gets the sum.
- `research_mail.bid_clauses` builds one pool and one plan per key from the tender Q&amp;A. A key with no matching mail is listed as a problem.
- In the orchestrator, `build_bid` and the blank-field check work on the lists. When calc fails for a pool or plan, `safe_calculate` drops one key at a time instead of all of them.

**Item 15: FX direction and metal-index unit**
- The FX record now reports the rate as written, which way it is quoted, and the amount if it is not 1 (e.g. "100 JPY = 0.62 EUR").
- A rate quoted as EUR per currency unit is used as before. A rate quoted as currency units per EUR goes into a new `fx_per_eur` input, and calc divides the price by it before rounding HALF_UP to cents (rule 22). Dividing matters: multiplying by a rounded reciprocal can land on the wrong side of a half cent, and a test shows such a case.
- Metal-index rates now report their mass unit (kg, 100 kg, tonne, gram) and are converted to EUR per kg exactly.

**Item 16: planner dropping a fact kind**
- The plan check in `plan_research` now sends the plan back if any of the four fact kinds has no site.
- If the plan is still incomplete after the retries, it asks once more without that check and fills the gaps with `complete_plan()`. The tender goes to the bid site, the mails to the mailbox site, and a missing specification or ERP to the sites no other kind holds.

**Item 20: approval sender**
- `sender_ok` now takes the email address out of strings like "Name &lt;address&gt;".
- A sender the brief names by name or role matches either the display name (whole words) or the address local part. It must also be on the bidder's mailbox domain when that domain is known. The other two sender forms keep the exact domain rule.

**Tests**
- `test_calc.py`: two pools with different cent rules, two bonus plans, old and new fields mixed, and the FX division cases.
- `test_research_mail.py`: FX in both directions, a per-tonne index, the sender rules, and pools and plans per key.
- `test_orchestrator.py`: `complete_plan`, and a failing pool that leaves the other pool in place.
- All tests use invented values.

Two things to know:
- When a pool can't be split for a position (e.g. no allocation mass), that position is still left out of the bid rather than losing only the pool. That was already the case before this change and I left it as is.
- FX values quoted per EUR and metal rates converted from another unit are posted to the verifier differently from what the mail says. The verifier could flag them as doubtful and send the mail search round again, costing some time on a private dataset. Public-1 doesn't hit this.</result>

<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jonasstenglein-OtherProjects-EHL-Finale-error404ai-ehl-finals/064ce75e-f288-40e2-8ed8-853ec224ce81/tasks/a7d100a6cd4c6623d.output</output-file>
