<summary>Agent "Tender Q&amp;A amendments reliability" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found why run_db71f7f475cb738b missed the amendment and fixed it. All three sandbox test runs of the fix scored 100, within main's usual 7 to 10 minutes. The work is committed on `worktree-agent-a7d100a6cd4c6623d`, not merged and not pushed.

**Cause of A.** The Q&amp;A paging (`collect_pages`) stopped one page early.
- On the public Q&amp;A the amendment for A-2 (stroke 482) is on page 5 of 6.
- After a click the list's inner frame was sometimes still loading. That view had no "Nächste Seite" control, so the paging took it as the end of the list. A local trace shows the same thing: 5 views collected, the last one an empty frame.
- The worker then opened the remaining pages itself until it hit its 28-request limit.
- The readers only read the collected pages, so the amendment was never seen. The unamended stroke plus the reserve gave 507 mm against the material's 500 mm, which became the no_bid.
- Nothing flagged the stop as a gap.
- The sandbox log no longer has the Q&amp;A worker's individual steps (the platform keeps only the last lines). So the sandbox cause is inferred from the request-limit stop plus the identical local trace.

**Changes**
- **Q&amp;A paging (`research_tender.py`):** it now waits for a frame that is still loading. When the tab shows no next control, it opens the clicked link on its own; if that page shows new answers, it keeps paging from there. If the page only repeats what was collected (the real end), it costs one extra page load. This only applies to the Q&amp;A list; the mail lists page as before.
- **Pages the worker opened itself:** after `collect_pages`, any new content the worker viewed is read too. Collapsed answers are expanded before reading.
- **Gap signal:** a Q&amp;A worker that stops before reaching the end of the list now raises a `tender/qa` gap, and the gap pass re-reads the Q&amp;A.
- **No_bid safety net (`orchestrator.py`):** before a no_bid for positions without a matching material, the Q&amp;A is re-read once on a fresh tab. The re-read is told which requirement and attribute missed (for example "A-2 (stroke)"); it gets at most 150 s, inside the time before the bid form. If it finds new amendments, those requirements get a new material choice and the bid is recalculated. Otherwise the no_bid stands.
- **Spec revisions are not re-read** by the safety net. Amendments live in the Q&amp;A, so it covers this failure.
- **B:** a website with Q&amp;A but no positions now returns its Q&amp;A instead of None. All tender reads (several websites, retries, re-checks) are combined: positions from the read with the most, answers from all. An answer already read keeps its first reading, so two websites no longer overwrite each other.
- **C:** stroke reserves are now keyed by reserve key and revision, using a new `calc.label_key` built from the existing `same_label` normaliser. A reserve that names no revision applies only when there is none for the requirement's own revision. In `research_mail.py` I only changed the reserve keying.

**Tests:** new `agent/tests/test_tender_qa.py` has 17 tests with invented values. They cover the paging fixes, reading pages the worker opened itself, merging reads across websites, the no_bid re-check, the `tender/qa` gap, and the reserve keying. The full suite passes (324 tests) after merging main twice, up to 91047a3. Both merges were clean, with no conflicts in `research_tender.py`, `orchestrator.py` or `research_mail.py`. I did not touch the gap-retry no_bid bug from run_83438e790be00c0d, as asked.

**Sandbox runs of 2e0ef68** (tested before the main merges, so they don't include the newer bid-form and mail-shape code):

| Run | Quality | Time | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| run_12eb930b93fd3fdb | 100 | 7:17 | $0.130 |
| run_3ded4f9ace4b9127 | 100 | 7:14 | $0.105 |
| run_eefe49a60acda07e | 100 | 9:16 | $0.150 |

All three read both A-2 amendments and the full Q&amp;A. In the first run a Q&amp;A page did not render after the click and was fetched by its link instead (`followed=1`). In the third run the Q&amp;A worker took 92 s, which made it the slowest of the three. The no_bid re-check never ran, since none of the runs reached a no_bid, so these runs add no time for it.

**Commits:** 2e0ef68 (the fix), two merges of main, and a82b8b7, which records the three runs in `experiments/runs.jsonl` and adds a `docs/learned.md` bullet on the cause.

<summary>Agent "Add Anthropic backend option" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>GLM 5.3 Flash through OpenRouter (pinned to Fireworks) scored 5 in all four sandbox runs. Every run sent `no_bid` on a bid scenario, so only the tender reference scored. Main's Qwen runs reach 100 on the same scenario, so the model is the cause, not the agent.

**Proxy facts (run-contract.md)**
- OpenRouter is a built-in provider: "`openrouter` | `https://openrouter.ai/api` | `$LLM_PROXY_URL/openrouter/v1`".
- Runs get no `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL`, so the code builds the URL from `LLM_PROXY_URL`.
- The key is sent as `Authorization: Bearer`. I checked it through the platform proxy with the real key: 200, served by Fireworks.
- On cost: "An upstream-reported USD cost takes precedence. OpenRouter reports one for every call (send `"usage": {"include": true}` with older clients)". We send it.

**What I built (on my branch, not merged or pushed)**
- `0fdfafb`: `LLM_BACKEND=openrouter`, with `LLM_MODEL` defaulting to `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`. It reuses the Qwen client path, so deadlines, the stream watchdog, retries and the `[model]` log all apply.
  - `LLM_PROVIDER` pins the serving provider: default `fireworks` with no fallbacks, and `any` lets OpenRouter choose.
  - Without `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` the run falls back to Qwen.
- **Thinking on:** `reasoning: {"effort": LLM_EFFORT}`, default medium.
- **Thinking off:** GLM 5.3 Flash refuses to turn reasoning off ("Reasoning is mandatory for this endpoint and cannot be disabled"). So off sends `{"effort": "low", "exclude": true}`, which measured 0 reasoning tokens on short answers.
- `a1db13f`: answers are capped at `LLM_MAX_TOKENS` (16000) and no temperature is sent (model default). This targets the 5-minute hangs described below.
- `agent/.env.example` and `agent/README.md` list the new variables. All 316 unit tests pass, including the new `tests/test_llm_openrouter.py`, which checks the request shape against a fake server.

**Local smoke test (direct to OpenRouter):** both modes returned the typed answer, served by Fireworks, in 1.3 s (thinking off) and 1.4 s (thinking on), each costing $0.0000375. OpenRouter reported `is_byok: false`, so the Fireworks key set up on openrouter.ai was not used and the calls ran on OpenRouter's own Fireworks access.

**Sandbox runs** (all four recorded in the main repo's `experiments/runs.jsonl`, not committed):

| Run | Commit | Quality | Time | Cost | p50 / p95 latency | Problems |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `run_bcc9a7a32d2da160` | 0fdfafb | 5 | 11:16 | $0.128 | 1.6 s / 5:01 | 15 calls hung 5 min |
| `run_111fb6e406222472` | 0fdfafb | 5 | 13:00 | $0.108 | 1.6 s / 7.3 s | 7 calls hung 5 min; the materials step finished only at 706 s |
| `run_fe95a4d0905342b9` | a1db13f, watchdog on | 5 | 10:06 | $0.140 | 1.6 s / 6.6 s | 42 × 429 rate limited |
| `run_0adaa2a208c0230d` | a1db13f, watchdog on | 5 | 8:01 | $0.150 | 1.4 s / 6.5 s | 73 × 429 rate limited |

Main's Qwen runs, for comparison: 6–9 min, $0.12–0.14, quality 100, p50 about 3.5 s.

**What failed and why**
- **No-bid decisions (the model):** in every run the per-end reserves for A-2 and A-4 stayed "pending" and material selection left A-2 (and in some runs A-1 or A-3) without a material. The agent therefore declared no_bid, which is worth 0 of the 75 decision points. In one run the end-reserve mail search hit its step budget. The decision itself is the agent's rules working as designed: they turn missing material choices into no_bid.
- **Hangs (the first two runs):** identical requests stalled until the proxy cut them at 5 minutes, with no usage reported, and repeated on each retry. With the stream watchdog on, the reruns had only 2–3 stalls, each cut within 20–60 s. I could not reproduce a hang locally, even with tools and large prompts.
- **429s (the reruns):** OpenRouter's shared Fireworks capacity rate-limited 42 and 73 calls, which pydantic-ai retried. Getting the Fireworks key itself used (BYOK) might avoid this.
- **Not the model:** the webmail sign-in timed out ("still loading") several times in run 3, a website problem. The two 401s at the end of runs 1 and 2 came after the bid was signed, when the run token closes.

**Cost:** Fireworks through OpenRouter charges $0.15 per million input tokens, $0.50 per million output tokens and $0.03 per million cached input tokens. Measured: $0.11–0.15 per run (650k–810k input with 90k–200k cached, 24k–52k output), about the same as Qwen. Prompt caching works but hit only 12–25 % of input.

GLM 5.3 Flash is not usable as the agent's model right now, and I see no further backend change that would fix the decision quality. Nothing else is running; no keys or secrets need setting.</result>

<result>Both bugs are fixed on `worktree-agent-acdfec2d75a7ea016` (head 7b5dc72, rebased on main b0eb1d4). Tests pass (327), and all six sandbox runs of the fixes that got as far as signing scored 100. One more run (ec4efc6c) produced a complete answer but was never signed because the tender portal page hung during the form.

**False no_bid in run 83438e79 (fixed in 940ac1d)**
- **(a) Cause:** the late tender positions did reach the recalculation (all 5 were in its input). The problem was A-2: its material was chosen as "none" at 234 s, while its stroke reserve was still pending. By the recalculation the reserve was known, so that stale "none" read as an established "no match", and calc answered no_bid.
- **Fix:** before every recalculation, the gap pass now chooses again every requirement that has no choice, from the facts as they are then.
- **Test:** a new test reproduces the run: no positions in the first pass, the gap round brings them, A-2 has the stale "none". It fails without the fix and passes with it.
- **(b) No positions never gives no_bid:** with no positions the pass already sends a bid with every position omitted. A no_bid can now only come from a choice made on current facts.
- **(c) Stopping with time left:** in this run the pass stopped because the false no_bid looked complete, so the stale choice was the cause. The second run (a75f0ec3, below) is a different cause of stopping early.
- **(d) The 28-request stop:** I could not find a cheap root cause. The recorded browser inputs show the tender worker clicking one spot four times at 19 to 25 s, then about 20 more short model calls with no clicks until it hit its limit at 58 s. The logs keep no step details, so I can't tell more. Mitigation instead: a tender attempt that ends without positions is now retried at once, not only after the whole research (in that run that would have been about 110 s earlier).

**Missing metal index in run a75f0ec3 (fixed in 248705d)**
- **Gap seen and retried:** yes. `mail/metal_indices` was detected at 270 s, covering all 5 positions, and retried.
- **Why it did not fill:** the message was found and read every time, but the reader copied only one of its three rate windows (valid to 24.01.2027, the day before the scenario date). So calc found no valid rate, and searching again just read the same partial record.
- **Why it ended with 7 minutes left:** a mail kind that had already read records only got 1 retry.
- **Fix:**
  - A real mail gap whose kind has read records is now first reread from the kept messages. The reader is told what the facts still lack: report every entry, key, rate and window, not only the first.
  - If the gap is still open, a later round searches again; such a kind now gets 2 tries instead of 1.
  - The record read later wins when the mail facts are resolved again.
  - A unit test covers it.

<result>My changes do not explain the 63.6 run, run_9b939cdb78646426. The Q&amp;A reader skipped three answers that were on the page it read, so 1af7d11 is not regressed by this branch. I added a guard against that skip anyway, and both sandbox runs of it scored 100. The fix is committed on my branch; nothing is merged or pushed.

**What happened in run_9b939cdb78646426:**
- **No merge involved.** There was a single tender read, so the cross-read merge returned it unchanged.
- **Paging was complete.** The Q&amp;A worker used "Weitere laden", which grows one list in place. A local re-run of that paging on the scouting site collected all 17 answers, and each kept view already contains the first page.
- **The reader dropped three answers.** Missing were the withdrawal of 8152 and the 01.01/01.02 packaging and commitment keys. Those explain 01.05 at 160 pieces, packaging 0 on 01.01 and 01.02, lead time 11 on 01.02, and the knock-on errors in the pool and bonus split.
- **This happened before my branch too.** run_c22233db5d990663 on 8cdb0cb shows the same pattern: withdrawn_answer 0 and packaging 0 on two positions.
- **Neither paging change ran here.** Following a link only applies to "Nächste Seite" links, and with "Weitere laden" the worker opened nothing new after collecting.
- **I can't fully prove which reader call dropped them.** The platform log keeps only the last lines and not the page views.

**run_a75f0ec37b475d89 (77) is a different failure.** Its Q&amp;A was right (withdrawn_answer full points); it lost the metal index on the mail side, so all five surcharges were blank. Its Q&amp;A worker hit the 28-request limit after 130 s, not 13 s. The same overrun happens on main before my branch (run_db71f7f475cb738b) and in my test runs. The worker keeps browsing after `collect_pages` until its step limit, which wastes model calls but does not change the answer.

**Fix, 9fb3be4:**
- **Answer check.** Each Q&amp;A reader now lists every answer number on the view. Any answer with no recorded fact triggers one more reading of that view, and the results are combined.
- **One "load more" view.** Each click grew the list but changed its button (moved, or shown as disabled), so three overlapping views were kept. Now the larger view replaces the smaller one; locally that turned 3 views into 1.
- **Merge order.** As you asked for item 3, when several reads are combined, the read that reached the end of the Q&amp;A, and then the one with more records, now wins for an answer both hold. The latest dated answer per key still wins, because the rules are applied after merging.
- **Tests.** Six new tests in `agent/tests/test_tender_qa.py` with invented values. All 329 unit tests pass.

| Run (9fb3be4) | Quality | Time | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| run_8417f9015452ca97 | 100 | 6:29 | $0.127 |
| run_fbe3b5d7628ee93b | 100 | 7:55 | $0.145 |

Both collected the Q&amp;A as a single complete view. A first reading that skips answers would print "recorded no fact" in the log, and neither run did, so the re-reading path has only been exercised by the unit test.

The runs are recorded in `experiments/runs.jsonl` and the analysis is in `docs/learned.md` (commit fa4e118).

Decision for you: should the Q&amp;A worker stop once `collect_pages` has reached the end? The tool's reply would tell it to return its summary at that point. That would save about 15 to 20 model calls per run. I haven't made or tested that change.</result>

<summary>Agent "Generalize bid form for private" finished</summary>
<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>Run ec4e failed because the bid form's tab stopped answering script calls and nothing ever replaced it. That is now fixed generically for every phase, not just the form. Main (972810c) is merged, the tests pass (341), and both sandbox runs scored quality 100. The branch is `worktree-agent-abf0de9d534fdb5a5`, head dd07a97, committed and not pushed.

**Cause, from the logs**
- **Run ec4e:**
  - At 441 s, the first choice click in the form timed out and the tab went dead.
  - Detection worked: each `settle()` reported "the page answers no script call", but nothing replaced the tab. Every later tool call waited out its timeouts (about 115 s each) on the same dead page, and the form pass used up its 496 s that way.
  - The first sign pass started at 976 s. The model navigated to the same URL, which brought the page back. The page hung again at 1047 s and the deadline hit at 1110 s, so the run never sent an answer.
- **Run d253:** same class. The ERP worker for HZ-2170 got the same dead-page report at 167 s. Its `open_pages` call then hung until 685 s, after a single model request.

**Fix**
- **Reopen a dead tab** (`browser_tools.py`): a new `reopen()` first opens the same URL in a new tab of the same browser context. That keeps the site's sign-in and the wizard's step on the server; the local test confirms the form comes back at the step it was on. If the new tab doesn't answer either, the site is started again in a fresh context and signed in anew, and a `restarts` counter is raised.
  - `settle()` and `look()` call it whenever the page stops answering.
  - Starts are left alone; they already handle hung pages themselves.
- **Cut off hung tool calls** (`worker.py`): every tool call of every worker is now watched (`watch_page`). If the worker's page misses script calls for 20 s, the call is cancelled, the page reopened, and the model gets the new view. A slow page that still answers is never cut off.
- **Form and signing** (`submit.py`):
  - The early bid-flow pass's session keep-alive (during research) reopens a dead tab right away and redoes its steps.
  - After a fresh-context restart, the positions counted as entered are cleared, so the review and totals match what the restarted form really holds. The earlier control mapping is kept for re-entering them.
  - Together with the repeating sign passes from before, a dead page now costs about 20 to 40 s instead of the rest of the run.

**Tests**
- New `tests/test_dead_page.py`: a local wizard server whose page is frozen mid-form.
  - A look reopens the form at step 2 within 40 s, keeping the session.
  - `settle()` recovers the same way.
  - When a new tab can't be opened, the site restarts in a fresh context.
  - A research tool stuck on a hung page is cut off and the page reopened, while a slow but answering tool runs to the end.
  - A restart clears the entered positions.
- I changed the existing frozen-page test in `tests/test_hang.py`: it now expects the reopen. The reopen is stubbed there, so the test no longer reaches example.com over the network.

**Sandbox runs** (commit 3aae806, experiment `dead-tab-recovery`, both recorded in `experiments/runs.jsonl`):

| Run | Quality | Research done | Form | Signed at |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 565c | 100 | 266 s | 40 s | 318 s |
| 8c1e | 100 | 313 s | 84 s | 424 s |

- **565c:** a mail tab hung mid-research and was reopened within about 1 s, and the run continued.
- **8c1e:**
  - At 36 s a tender-portal research worker's tab reported a dead page, but no reopen followed.
  - My guess is that the site's start was still running at that moment, since reopen deliberately stays out of starts; I did not confirm this.
  - The run reached 100 anyway.

**Earlier in this task:** the form generalization and signing work reported before is all on this branch. Its last two sandbox runs (0b23 and 1124) also scored 100.

I touched `browser_tools.py` and `worker.py`, which other agents work on; the changes are additive, but they need a look when merging. I added a bullet to `docs/learned.md`.
